# Decisions log

One line of reasoning per decision. Code comments cite entries by number, for example `D-018`.
Numbers are never reused; entries that only concerned the development machine are not listed.

**The standing rule (2026-09-29, CLAUDE.md §9):** anything reversible in under an hour is decided, built and
recorded here — the date, the choice, the reason, the commit — without asking first. Between two behaviours
that already disagree, the one used elsewhere in the product wins. Asked first are only: the irreversible (data
loss, money moving, a production deploy), what needs the operator's credential, file or purchase, what
contradicts CLAUDE.md, and what changes what a customer is told about the legality of a wheel on their car.

| # | Date | Decision | Reasoning |
|---|---|---|---|
| D-004 | 2026-09-20 | **Redis and Horizon run on the Linux host only.** Local cache, queue and session use the `database` drivers; `predis/predis` is installed so `redis` works in production without a PHP extension. | `laravel/horizon` needs `ext-pcntl` and `ext-posix`, which Windows lacks. Nothing in the domain code depends on the driver. |
| D-007 | 2026-09-20 | **No Tailwind.** Removed from the scaffold together with `@tailwindcss/vite`. | The design system is the two ported stylesheets, `tokens.css` and `components.css`; Lato and IBM Plex Mono load from Google Fonts. |
| D-009 | 2026-09-20 | **Tests run against a real MySQL database (`rimify_test`)**, never SQLite in memory. | The engine's SQL uses window functions, `SIGNAL` triggers, CHECK constraints and FULLTEXT; SQLite would silently pass code that fails on MySQL — exactly the "reads correct and is wrong" class of defect. |
| D-010 | 2026-09-20 | **Strict mode is asserted at boot** in `AppServiceProvider` on every MySQL connection, throwing if a required `sql_mode` flag is absent. | A missing flag would let R-03 fail open. |
| D-011 | 2026-09-20 | **`ONLY_FULL_GROUP_BY` is kept** in the connection's `modes`. | It is the MySQL 8.4 default and it catches an aggregate query silently returning arbitrary rows. |
| D-012 | 2026-09-20 | **Inertia v3** (`inertia-laravel` 3.3, `@inertiajs/vue3` 3.7). | It is what Composer resolves against Laravel 13, and server and client adapters must share a major version. |
| D-013 | 2026-09-20 | **Migrations are grouped by bounded context** in `database/migrations/{fitment,commerce,vehicle_import,integration}`, registered with `loadMigrationsFrom()`; ordering stays global by filename. | One context can be migrated and tested alone (`RIMIFY_MIGRATION_SLICE`), and 40+ migrations stay navigable. |
| D-014 | 2026-09-20 | **Edge case E3 ships as the literal Figma rule** — a stored vehicle that has never entered the booking process shows a plain header — behind one constructor flag on `HeaderModeResolver`. | Implement what was drawn; keep the more useful variant one line of configuration away for design review. |
| D-015 | 2026-09-20 | **Tablets receive the desktop layout.** `Sec-CH-UA-Mobile` outranks the User-Agent; `?view=desktop\|mobile` pins a layout in a 7-day cookie and `?view=auto` releases it. | The mobile documents are designed for 390px with a thumb-reach bottom nav; a 768px+ viewport is served better by the desktop document. |
| D-016 | 2026-09-20 | **The audit trail is `spatie/laravel-activitylog` with its table renamed `audit_logs`**, extended with `ip_address`, `correlation_id` and `actor_email`, and made append-only by triggers. | One table instead of two competing logs. |
| D-017 | 2026-09-20 | **`App\Domain\Fitment\Derivation\ReferenceTables` is the single source** for the load-index and speed-symbol figures; the database tables are seeded from it and a test asserts they are equal. | The compliance owner can inspect real tables, and the seed and the test oracle cannot drift apart. |
| D-018 | 2026-09-21 | **The speed-symbol derivation follows the stated rule. `P` is correct for 148 km/h.** | The spec's worked example printed `T` because its table omitted N, P, Q, R, S and U, so `T` was the first symbol still present — a defect in the example, not the rule. The complete scale (`L M N P Q R S T U H V W Y (Y)`) is seeded, `SpeedSymbolDeriver` is asserted at every boundary, and a test proves every symbol is reachable, so a truncated table can never pass unnoticed. |
| D-019 | 2026-09-21 | **A safety margin above the derived legal minimum is configuration, not data:** `config/rimify.php` → `speed_symbol_margin_steps` and `load_index_margin_steps`, both `0` by default. | Hard-coding a margin would make a commercial choice indistinguishable from the legal figure. A margin may only raise the requirement — a negative value throws. |
| D-020 | 2026-09-21 | **The supersession chain is guarded in three layers:** the model, a `BEFORE UPDATE` trigger raising `SQLSTATE 45000`, and `SupersedeChain` for cycles. | MySQL refuses a CHECK on an `AUTO_INCREMENT` column and `BEFORE INSERT` cannot see `NEW.id`, so no single layer covers every path. A chain too deep to verify is refused rather than followed. |
| D-021 | 2026-09-21 | **The four `RIMIFY-GARANTIE` basket rows are** `Garantierte Passgenauigkeit` · `Gutachten zu jeder Felge` · `Montiert und gewuchtet` · `Express-Versand aus Deutschland`. **Client confirmation pending.** | They mirror the hero trust strip, so the promise made above the fold is repeated at the basket. |
| D-022 | 2026-09-21 | **Where the FAQ sources differ, the Copy Pack wins** (Q2, Q3, Q6, Q7, Q8). | The design specification is the earlier draft. Q1, Q4 and Q5 are identical in both. |
| D-023 | 2026-09-21 | **Contact details live once, in `config/rimify.php`**, and every appearance renders from there. **Client confirmation pending.** | The homepage and the Kontakt page carried different numbers. Tests police the canonical values and forbid a hard-coded phone or e-mail in any renderable file. |
| D-024 | 2026-09-21 | **Legal pages ship with real section headings and a visibly marked placeholder body** — `[Rechtstext folgt von der Kanzlei — Abschnitt: <heading>]`. | German legal text is drafted by the client's Kanzlei, not by developers. The page is honest about what is missing. |
| D-026 | 2026-09-21 | **R-03 outranks R-06.** A vehicle whose axle loads or top speed could not be parsed yields `UNKNOWN` even when the Gutachten states its own minimum. | A document minimum says what the *wheel* requires, not what the *car* can carry. Honouring it against an uncharacterised vehicle would be a positive verdict with the physics check skipped. |
| D-027 | 2026-09-21 | **An unrecognised speed symbol printed by a document is ignored, not trusted.** The derivation governs alone. | A symbol absent from `speed_symbol_table` has no rank; treating it as a minimum would compare as "no requirement" and relax the answer in the permissive direction. |
| D-028 | 2026-09-21 | **Storefront photography is placeholder imagery served from our own origin** (`public/images`). | Third-party download endpoints are slow and rate-limited, and Unsplash is not a licence RIMIFY holds. The client's own hero and product photography replaces these before launch. |
| D-029 | 2026-09-21 | **The storefront renders from Vue components on the design system; the prototype runtime is retired.** | The first preview reproduced a prototype pixel for pixel, and the client's verdict was that it looked self-made and misbehaved on phones. A page drawn at runtime with inline styles cannot be made responsive or polished without wrapping the prototype's own code. The Vue implementation renders the same data through components, so layout, motion and states are ours to refine. |
| D-030 | 2026-09-26 | **The admin panel is light. The rail is `--c-surface` with a 1px `--c-line` right edge, and no admin surface is dark by default.** A dark admin, if the client ever asks for one, is a full dark theme across every admin surface with its own validated token set and chart palette, decided as its own piece of work — never approximated with a permanent black column. | The admin is a workspace people sit in for hours; the dark band is a storefront device for emphasis. Spending it on furniture buys nothing and costs a second dark variant of every component that can appear in 260px of screen — count badge, environment badge, focus ring, hover, flyouts. The reference set (Shopify, Linear, Atlassian, Stripe, Microsoft) is light for the same reason. Brand presence in the admin comes from the wordmark, the blue active state and the typography, not from a black column. |
| D-031 | 2026-09-29 | **The decision log stays `docs/decisions.md` (lower case).** The standing rule's `docs/DECISIONS.md` is this file. | README, CONTRIBUTING, three tests and a domain class link it by that name; between two spellings, the one already used wins (CLAUDE.md §9). — this commit |
| D-032 | 2026-09-29 | **The 1er body-name conflict is DATA-CONFLICTS Nr. 5**, not „conflict 2“ as the prompt named it. | Nr. 2 was taken (Wintertauglichkeit); numbers are never reused there either. — `136be8b5` |
| D-033 | 2026-09-29 | **The per-document term map is each body code's list of customer body styles** (`DocumentCondition.bodyForms`, entered with the code); the Nachtrag's „Kombilimousine“ admits Schrägheck, 55255's `Car` admits Kombi. Door counts are not asked. | The map already existed in that column; only one entry was wrong. BMW's types fix the doors (1K2 3-door, 1K4 5-door) and 187's rows print both door codes, so doors narrow no row of either document. — `136be8b5` |
| D-034 | 2026-09-29 | **A body style or drive is derived only from a whole word** of model or variant (quattro, xDrive — also as a word's start, „xDrive30d“ —, 4MATIC, 4x4, Allrad; Touring, Avant, Kombi; Coupé/Coupe, Cabrio, Cabriolet, Roadster); two words pointing two ways derive nothing. | „4er-GranCoupé“ is a four-door car and „Allradlenkung“ steers, it does not drive: a substring match would have answered both wrongly. — `f27d9a8d` |
| D-035 | 2026-09-29 | **The vehicle data's value stands; a gap is filled from the owner's answer, else from the name — in the one vehicle repository, the admin's preview included.** Engine 1.2.0. | One place answers every surface (R-13); the owner knows their car better than a word of its name; the version names the build that could answer differently. — `f27d9a8d` |
| D-036 | 2026-09-29 | **A choice in the body/drive question is sent at once**; the vaguer reason line gives way to the question while it stands. | „Trag sie ein und wir prüfen sofort“ promises no second step; the question says by name what the reason only hinted at. — `19ab5335` |
| D-037 | 2026-09-29 | **Shop rate limits: the visitor key lives a year in the encrypted `rmf_geraet` cookie; the address fallback is ten times the ceiling; the logged address is anonymised** (IPv4 /24, IPv6 /48). | A key that expires with the session would send a returning buyer back to the shared address; ten times covers a carrier's evening; the log needs to show a shared address being turned away, not to follow a person. — `60b8d7d0` |
| D-038 | 2026-09-29 | **The recent-car chip carries Feld K's range quieter beside the short name**, and in its accessible name. | The two halves of a split car otherwise read alike; the chip stays one line. — `84fefd68` |
| D-039 | 2026-09-29 | **The header search counts on a limiter of its own, 120 a minute per visitor**, apart from the vehicle picker and the live count (60). | A search sends a request per keystroke that follows the last by 120 ms: at ordinary typing speed 60 ran out mid-sentence and took the picker down with it (EF-04). — `4ccb295a` |
| D-040 | 2026-09-29 | **„Antriebsart“, not „Antrieb“, where the shop asks for it**; the operator's Karosserieform sentence is kept word for word, and the drive's follows it. The admin keeps „Antrieb“ (the documents' word). | „Für dieses Fahrzeug fehlt der Antrieb“ reads as a car without a drivetrain (copy-editor-de). — this commit |
| D-041 | 2026-09-29 | **Where an answer waits only for the body style or drive, the panel's headline reads „Noch offen für …“**, not „Keine Freigabe hinterlegt für …“; the state stays `UNKNOWN`, nothing is sold, the listing's count is unchanged. | The operator's ruling on EF-01 — say which field is missing and what it unlocks —, and the two lines contradicted each other: a document for the car is on file, the car's answer is not given yet. — this commit |
| D-042 | 2026-09-29 | **The body-style drawings stay in the question, drawn as the icon set is drawn** (1,5 px, square ends, mitred corners, a stroke that does not scale), not moved into `resources/js/icons/`. | The set is 24 × 24 glyphs; a car's side view needs 48 × 24, and nothing else uses them. — this commit |
| D-043 | 2026-09-29 | **The address ceiling (×10) stands over every shop request, keyed or not**; the visitor key is counted in addition. | A key costs nothing: a client that never sends `rmf_geraet` back got a fresh allowance with every page load, so „Sag mir Bescheid“ mailed without bound (G-06). The ×10 is the operator's own figure for an address. Logged once a minute per address and limiter (G-09). — this commit |
| D-044 | 2026-09-29 | **The checkout stays unlimited by address, also for a request without a key** (G-08, left as is). | The operator's ruling: „exempt the checkout path entirely from IP-based limiting“. Harmless while `/kasse` takes no real payment; to be looked at again with Block G (Stripe). — this commit |
| D-045 | 2026-09-29 | **No body style is read from „Coupé“ in a four-door's name** (BMW Gran Coupé, Mercedes CLA/CLS Coupé, „4-Türer“), **and no word right after „ohne“, „nicht“, „kein“** (G-03). | Those words name a Fließheck or rule the drive out; read as said, they made rows printed „nur Coupé“ or „nur mit Allradantrieb“ PERMITTED for cars they do not cover. — this commit |
| D-046 | 2026-09-29 | **The listing asks only for what its current page's cards wait for** (G-13, left as is). | The answers are computed for the cards shown; a car's answer, once given, holds for every page. The next page asks what it needs. — this commit |
| D-047 | 2026-09-29 | **On the shared host the renderer listens on a Unix socket (`storage/framework/ssr.sock`, 0600), not on a TCP port**; PHP reaches it through curl's socket option. Local development keeps the port. | Measured on the preview: a port a customer's process opens is never reachable there — Inertia's renderer, a bare Node server and PHP's own server all said „listening“ and all answered „connection refused“, and no page was ever server-rendered. A socket file answered at once — and is this account's alone, where 127.0.0.1 is shared with every site on the machine. — this commit |
| D-048 | 2026-09-29 | **`remote.sh` takes `RIMIFY_CRON=manual`: the two cron lines are printed for hPanel instead of written with `crontab`.** | Writing the account's crontab from a release script is persistence the operator adds by hand (GO-LIVE §6), not something a deploy slips in. — this commit |
| D-049 | 2026-09-29 | **Withdrawn the same day:** the preview's example catalogue is drafts only (release seed v4), so no demo pair is public either; the smoke keeps asking for a real one. ~~Outside production, `rimify:smoke` falls back to the example catalogue when no real wheel is released yet, and says so in its rows**; a real pair always goes first, production never falls back. | On the preview the only real wheel (DB8GT2) waits for the operator's release and price, so the product page without JavaScript, the lookup, the PDF and the test order could not be checked at all; production holds no demo row (R-12) and must still prove a real one.~~ — `50ef0d0b`, taken back in the next commit |
| D-050 | 2026-09-29 | **GO-LIVE §7.2 loads the dump with its triggers' DEFINER clauses removed, and stops before 7.4 on a differing trigger count.** | Measured on the host: a shared MariaDB account lacks SET USER; the first rehearsal's load stopped at the first trigger and 7.4 opened the site on half a database. Without the clause every trigger belongs to the same account. — this commit |
| D-051 | 2026-09-29 | **`npm run qa` leaves the product routes out, and says so, where a shop has no public product.** | The preview has none until DB8GT2 is released and priced; measuring a 404 as the product page would report a pass that is not one. — this commit |
| D-052 | 2026-09-29 | **The suite runs on the host's engine, MariaDB 11.8, by default, and on MySQL 8.4 as well; the app refuses any engine the suite has not run against, at the first connection, naming both** (`DatabaseEngine`, `rimify.database.engines`, CI with one leg each — a test keeps the list and the matrix equal). An engine is compared by release series (11.8, 8.4), not major number. The strict-mode check now also runs for Laravel's `mariadb` driver. | The preview host is MariaDB 11.8.9 (`SELECT VERSION()`, 29.09.) while every test had run on MySQL 8.4.9 — D-009's risk one layer up. Measured: CHECK, SIGNAL, FULLTEXT, collation and strict mode behave alike; the whole suite passed on MariaDB but for one test reading `information_schema`'s spelling (fixed). Series, because two MariaDB 11 minors differ more than their shared major suggests. CLAUDE.md §4 still names MySQL 8.4 only — for the operator to amend. — this commit |
| D-053 | 2026-09-29 | **Gutachten › Abdeckung lists the released documents without a table of tyre load at speed** („Reifen nur bis 210 km/h“), beside the Nachträge without their base. | The Nachtrag prints none (pages 7–17 read again from the PDF's text): every car over 210 km/h gets no tyre answer on the 18″, with its sentence. The gap belongs where the people who run the shop look, not only in a fast car's answer. — this commit |
| D-054 | 2026-09-29 | **The webhook's work runs right after the 200 where the queue is `sync` (the host has no worker), and on the queue everywhere else.** Every event is stored raw first under its unique id; a replay is answered 200 and does nothing. | Stripe waits for an answer; a job run inside the request would make it wait for the order's mail and invoice. `afterResponse()` keeps the answer fast without a worker the shared host cannot supervise. — this commit |
| D-055 | 2026-09-29 | **Stock is taken off the shelf when the order is written and put back when it dies unpaid (storniert, abgelaufen after 24 h); a declined payment keeps it for the retry.** An order moves only as `OrderStatus::next()` allows, enforced in the model; every move is audited with the provider's event id. | Reserving at the payment's success would sell the last set twice while two customers type their card numbers; releasing on a decline would take the customer's wheels away between two attempts a minute apart. — this commit |
| D-056 | 2026-09-29 | **The Stripe checkout writes the order only at „Zahlungspflichtig bestellen“, after Stripe has checked its own fields, and compares what the page showed (`CheckoutShown`: each verdict, its Auflagen and Gutachten revisions; each price and the totals) with the basket as it is then.** A changed verdict or Gutachten stops the payment with its own sentence, a changed price with the basket's; a second press with the same data reuses the order and its PaymentIntent; changed data cancels the unpaid order and writes a new one. | The brief's re-validation at payment time; Stripe's deferred-intent flow, so no order and no intent exist for a customer who only looks. — this commit |
| D-057 | 2026-09-29 | **A test order (the sandbox, Stripe in Testmodus) may go before the shipping price and the delivery time are named, and records that on the order; a real payment waits for both.** The delivery time is the operator's own words (`RIMIFY_DELIVERY_TIME`). | The preview must be able to take a test card end to end while the client's figures are outstanding; a live shop must never ask for money without them. — this commit |
| D-058 | 2026-09-29 | **Hausnummer opens the number pad, with „Mit Buchstaben, z. B. 12a“ one tap below it; a street autofilled with its number („Musterstraße 12a“) gives the number to its own field while that is empty.** No autocomplete token exists for a separate house number, so the field asks for none. | The brief asks for number pads; the iPhone's has no letters, and 12a is an everyday German address. — this commit |
| D-059 | 2026-09-29 | **The PLZ fills Ort only when exactly one place answers and Ort is empty; several are offered as buttons; nothing typed is ever overwritten.** GeoNames (CC BY 4.0), Großkunden left out; the source is named beside every suggestion and in `docs/data-credits.md`. | A lookup that corrects the customer would be the checkout being confidently wrong about their own address. — this commit |
| D-060 | 2026-09-29 | **The §14 invoice is issued when the payment is confirmed, the confirmation mail goes out with it (AGB, Widerrufsbelehrung and the Muster-Widerrufsformular in the mail's text, the invoice attached), and the order page names the mail only once it was sent.** Leistungszeitraum is the calendar month; test orders and an incomplete seller get the TEST series. | § 312f BGB wants the terms on a durable medium with the contract; a TEST series keeps the real numbering unbroken for the first real sale. The tax adviser confirms the layout (BLOCKED 19). — this commit |
| D-061 | 2026-09-29 | **Stripe's origins are admitted on the checkout alone** (script, connect and frame, as Stripe publishes them), with `payment` delegated to its frames and COOP relaxed to `same-origin-allow-popups` there only. The billing address sits behind an unchecked box („Rechnung an eine andere Adresse“). | TTDSG: no third-party script where it is not needed; a wallet's pop-up needs its opener. — this commit |
| D-062 | 2026-09-29 | **`rmf_papiere` and `rmf_geraet` are listed as functional cookies, each with what it stores and how to clear it, from one list (`resources/js/legal/cookies.ts`) that the cookie settings and a facts table on the Datenschutz page both print.** The table stands beside the Kanzlei's text, never in its place. | The brief's §3; one list so the two surfaces cannot disagree; D-024 keeps the legal prose the Kanzlei's. — this commit |
| D-063 | 2026-09-29 | **GPSR data is entered per brand (Katalog › Marken › Herstellerangaben) and printed on every product page of the brand; without it the page says the details are still to come, the wheel stays sellable, and the preflight names the brand.** | The manufacturer is a property of the brand, not of each size; the details are the client's and the manufacturer's to give — the shop never fills them in. Blocking the sale would be a fitment-style refusal for a labelling gap. — this commit |
| D-064 | 2026-09-29 | **The home hero's price carries „inkl. MwSt., zzgl. Versand“ under it, desktop and phone.** | PAngV: wherever a price stands, what it includes stands beside it; the footer's line alone is too far from a price in the hero. The protected hero keeps every element; one line is added. — this commit |
| D-065 | 2026-09-29 | **A refund is made from the order page only through the provider's API, behind the password again (sudo), and is written back at once**: the order becomes teilweise erstattet or erstattet, a credit note in its own series documents it, the customer gets it by mail, the Protokoll names who asked. A pending refund waits for the provider's word; the webhook's `charge.refunded` after an immediate one finds nothing new. | Money leaving the account is the one admin action that cannot be undone; a refund recorded by hand would be a credit note without money or money without a credit note. — this commit |
| D-066 | 2026-09-29 | **Chargebacks live under Verkauf › Rückbuchungen** (not „Konflikte“, which is the Gutachten's), open ones first with the days to the evidence deadline counted down (warned from three), and are answered once — an explanation, up to two files, the order's invoice as the receipt — through the API. | Stripe takes one submission per dispute; a deadline nobody sees is money gone. — this commit |
| D-067 | 2026-09-29 | **A key-number lookup that finds nothing says „… haben wir die Daten für dieses Fahrzeug noch nicht hinterlegt.“** on every selector and in the verdict reason, and hands back nothing but what was typed. | The vehicle dataset is the client's to supply (BLOCKED 16): the car exists, our list does not have it yet — „kein Fahrzeug gefunden“ read as if the numbers were wrong. | D-068 | 2026-09-29 | **The account after the order is one click on the paid order's confirmation page: it opens an account under the order's address and mails a one-time link to set the password.** Its own guard and table, keyed by e-mail; it lists every order of its address; deleting it leaves the orders. | D-070 | 2026-09-30 | **The copy review's findings are taken whole**; where one needed a person, it went to BLOCKED 19 instead of being guessed: the model form's addressee (name and postal address) and the invoice's Leistungszeitraum (the tax adviser). | D-071 | 2026-09-30 | **The look review's fixes are taken for everything Block G built; its findings on older, approved parts of the checkout and the order page are left**: the legacy field classes and the form's frame, the green done-steps and tick, the red required asterisks. | Review triage: the reviewer governs craft on new work; restyling an approved page is a task of its own, not a side effect of a payments block. — this commit |
| D-072 | 2026-09-30 | **The scheduler's pulse lives in the `settings` table, not the cache**, and the admin's health line has a row „Zeitplan“: amber, with a banner, after 60 minutes without a pulse — at once where it never ran — on production and staging; elsewhere it stays quiet. The queue's banner is the pattern already in the product for a background process silent for an hour. — this commit |
| D-073 | 2026-09-30 | **The real invoice numbers are opened once, in Einstellungen › Zahlung, by the Super-Admin after the password**: both first numbers typed from the client's accounting (no default), a check box for the comparison, the operator named on the row and in the Protokoll. The number format stays `RE-` + at least six digits. | The operator's condition: one-way, audited, a number from the client, not 1. The switch sits beside the keys it belongs with, behind the same sudo. — this commit |
| D-074 | 2026-09-30 | **The database holds the series by itself**: a real series row can only be inserted opened; a counter moves by exactly +1 and nothing else in the row changes; no row is deleted; an invoice takes exactly the number its series just counted to; CHECKs spell the number from series and sequence, so `TEST-` exists only in a T-series. A migration that finds a real series used before any opening stops rather than invent an operator. | The operator asked for proof that no TEST number can reach the real series and that it is gapless from its first issue; the code alone could be bypassed. — this commit |
| D-075 | 2026-09-30 | **Kleinunternehmer is one flag, `RIMIFY_SELLER_SMALL_BUSINESS`**: it sets the rate every new order is written at (0 or 19 %); the invoice, the order page, the mail and the admin read the order's own frozen rate, never today's flag; the site's price notes read it through one composable (`usePriceTerms`). Wording: beside a price „zzgl. Versand, keine MwSt. nach § 19 UStG“; the footer and the invoice „Gemäß § 19 UStG wird keine Umsatzsteuer berechnet.“ | The operator's condition: a setting, not a rewrite, changing invoice, price display and order summary together; an order already placed keeps what it was sold at. — this commit |
| D-076 | 2026-09-30 | **The PAY-01 regression runs the whole cycle — reserve, decline, retry, succeed — on both paths, with the last four wheels on the shelf**, and ends with `orders:expire` 25 hours later to prove nothing is handed back from a paid order. The e2e spec pays at the end instead of cancelling. | The operator's note: the count alone proved the retry was possible, not that it ends in a paid order. — this commit |
| D-077 | 2026-09-30 | **No fallback for the invoice numbers: production does not go live until the operator has opened them.** If the client has a series running, its next value is entered as is. If it has none, the numbers follow the German convention — `RMF-2026-00001` for invoices, `RMF-G-2026-00001` for credit notes, a counter per series and year, gapless within the year — built when that answer arrives, not before. | The operator's rule of 30.09.: the preflight refusing is exactly right; a counter per year keeps yearly bookkeeping legible and the gapless requirement manageable per year. Building the yearly format now would guess the client's answer. — this commit |
| D-078 | 2026-09-30 | **An order keeps the VAT rate it was sold at.** The Kleinunternehmer flag decides the rate of orders written from now on; an order already placed — its page, its mail, its invoice, a later credit note — reads the rate frozen on its own lines, whatever the flag says when they are made. Until the tax adviser answers, the flag stays at normal VAT. | Switching the flag must never rewrite what a customer was sold: the invoice has to match the price paid and the tax it contained on that day. — this commit |
| D-079 | 2026-09-30 | **A browser test reads the result of a server round trip only once the page says the round trip is over** — a control the page disables while it waits enabled again, the focus landed where the page puts it — never at a fixed time after the request. **And every browser run starts from the same shop**: both Playwright configs rebuild the e2e shop's database before the run (`e2e/global-setup.ts`, ≈20 s, `E2E_KEEP_SHOP=1` skips it). `E2E_CPU_THROTTLE` slows the pages for a stress run. | K2-01 passed alone and failed in the suite: it read the rename's outcome 800 ms after the PUT, and on a loaded machine the page came later (reproduced under load, 2 of 5; 10 of 10 after the fix). The specs that buy left the shop poorer with every run. — this commit |
| D-080 | 2026-10-01 | **The preview is gated in two layers with one password**: a basic-auth middleware ahead of everything Laravel answers (pages, admin, maintenance page), and a basic-auth `.htaccess` in `storage/app/public` for the files the web server serves itself (the Gutachten PDFs, the pictures), written by `rimify:preview-gate` on every deploy. Both live where `git reset --hard` never reaches (`.env`, `storage/`). Stripe's webhook, `/up` and robots.txt stay open. Every copy but the live shop sends `X-Robots-Tag: noindex, nofollow` and a robots.txt that disallows everything (the static file became a route). A slim ribbon says it is a preview until put away (a cookie the server reads, so the first frame is right). The Impressum prints the seller data under „Angaben gemäß § 5 DDG“, what is missing marked as missing. The password lives in `.env` and in the git-ignored `docs/review/ACCOUNTS.local.md`, never in the repository. | The operator's rule: a publicly reachable German shop front owes an Impressum, and the preview's legal texts are placeholders. A gate in the tracked `.htaccess` would be reset by every deploy; one in the app alone would leave the PDFs open, since `/storage` never reaches Laravel. — this commit |
| D-081 | 2026-10-01 | **Two questions about the papers, two lines, one place for their words** (`PapersWording`, its TS twin `resources/js/fitment/papers.ts`). The Auflagen decide whether a Prüfer must see the car (an Abnahme code, as before); the document decides whether the papers must be corrected (§ 15 FZV, a fact on the record: „Berichtigung der Fahrzeugpapiere“ — nicht erforderlich / erforderlich / steht nicht im Dokument). Where a document frees the papers, the first line names the Abnahme as what it is — „Abnahme durch einen Prüfer erforderlich (§ 19 Abs. 3 StVZO).“ — and a second line says „Keine Berichtigung der Fahrzeugpapiere nötig (§ 15 FZV).“; where it says nothing, the words stay as they were. Both lines are frozen with the verdict and read back by the order page, the mail, the basket and the admin. | ABE 53841 point 10 frees the papers, point 11 leaves the Änderungsabnahme to the Prüfbericht: the operator's „two different questions, two different lines“. „Eintragung … erforderlich“ beside „keine Berichtigung“ would contradict itself. — this commit |
| D-082 | 2026-10-01 | **A Nachtrag carries its base's § 15 FZV only once the base is released**; the record says the base's word is waiting for that release. A base whose Verwendungsbereich stands in its Nachtrag's Anlagen (ABE 53841: „Anlage/n zum Prüfbericht 1 - 16“) has no rows of its own and may be released without rows, for its facts — any other document without a row still may not. | A fact nobody has checked against its PDF never reaches a customer; releasing is the user's act (the drafts rule). Without the exception the base could never be released and its point 10 would never count. — this commit |
| D-083 | 2026-10-01 | **„Fehlende Anlagen“ is a fact on the record, and it turns a refusal into UNKNOWN only where the pages we hold are silent.** A car no row of the incomplete document names gets `ANNEX_MISSING`: „Für dieses Fahrzeug liegt uns die entsprechende Anlage des Gutachtens noch nicht vor. Bis dahin können wir die Freigabe nicht bestätigen.“ A car the pages we hold name and rule out (window, geometry, body style) stays NOT_PERMITTED — the document spoke. The record and Gutachten › Abdeckung say „Verwendungsbereich unvollständig – Anlagen 1–15 liegen nicht vor.“ | R-07: the Anlage that names the car may be among the fifteen we lack — „we have not read that page“, never „no approval“. — this commit |

| D-085 | 2026-10-01 | **The seller and the shipping live in Einstellungen › Verkäufer & Versand, one settings row each; `.env` answers only until the page is saved** — then the page's word wins, a cleared field included (the pattern of the Komplettrad prices). The invoice starting numbers keep their own one-way screen under Zahlung; the Kleinunternehmer flag stays in `.env` (a tax decision for a deploy, not a field). | The operator's §2: the client enters it in the admin, from one place for the Impressum, the invoice, the mail, the product page, the basket and the checkout. — this commit |
| D-086 | 2026-10-01 | **„Platzhalter“ is a mark per section, and a placeholder is never said to a customer as if it were real**: the Impressum alone shows it, under „Platzhalter: Die folgenden Angaben sind Beispielwerte …“; the invoice, the Widerrufsformular, the mail, the product page, the basket and the checkout treat it as not given (a test invoice, „Versandkosten werden noch festgelegt“, no real order); the preflight refuses the live shop while one is left. | The client asked for „generic things“ now and the real data in the live shop; failing closed to „not given“ is the path the shop already has for a missing value. — this commit |
| D-087 | 2026-10-01 | **Germany only, deliberately — and the country field arrives together with OSS, never before.** Shipping is priced per delivery country (the data allows more), but the page offers Germany alone and the checkout asks for no country. Delivering to Austria or the Netherlands is not a shipping-cost question but a VAT one: cross-border sales to consumers above the EU-wide threshold (10.000 € a year) fall under the OSS regime — the VAT rate of the destination country on every order, OSS registration and returns, and an invoice that names the rate and country it applies. That is its own piece of work (BLOCKED, future scope); a country dropdown added without it would charge German VAT on a foreign sale. The delivery time is a range of working days (`3–5 Werktage`). The shipping methods the client once named (Standard / Premium, client-questions.md) are not built by this: one price per country is what the brief asks now. The product page shows `Lieferzeit … · Versand … · versandkostenfrei ab …` under the price, only what is given. The seller's line (with Geschäftsführung and register, § 35a GmbHG) stands under every invoice and the order mail. | The operator's note of 01.10.2026: record the reasoning, so the limitation reads as a decision and nobody adds a dropdown in six months and creates a tax problem. — this commit |

A pair is offered where the table lists it front first and the car's row lists both sizes on their axles, and only once the car's axle loads and top speed settled every axle (`TyreCheck::figuresPermitted`); each axle's tyre is measured against that axle's own load, and the size's own load-index limits (T95 …) are read at that load, never another size's. Front and rear are one maker's one line in one season, as V19 asks. A mixed set is one basket entry shown as two lines (2 + 2 on one rim size; quantity, removal and colour change both) and two order lines in one package group with `order_lines.axle`, each freezing its axle, size, partner size, table entry, page and minimum (`verdict.mixedTyres`). It never reads „nur Hinweise“; the table's own sentence is quoted beside it. **Rule 2 of the Komplettrad eligibility now refuses only the tyre rules the engine cannot read itself**: a parsed pairing table does not stop four identical tyres (it speaks only of different sizes), an axle code with its axle makes the size axle-scoped (rule 3b), and a load-index limit naming its index is applied per size — but only from the car's own figures; without them it still refuses. The staggered refusal no longer says „stellen wir persönlich zusammen“. | Combining freely is how a 255/40 ends up at the front of a car whose document allows it only at the rear. Without the rule-2 change the page would refuse four identical tyres with „das Gutachten schreibt bestimmte Reifen vor“ and offer a mixed set below it — two answers about one document. The axle requirement merges every size's limits, which for one size can read a lower index than its own codes allow; the per-size reading closes that from the safe side. — this commit |

The key-number route is now first in the markup, „oder“ second, the drill third, and the layout only spaces them — no `order` left; from 900 px the same order stands in three columns (keys left, makes right), and the selector page's lead names the routes in that order. | D-139 | 2026-10-02 | **The hero takes the blue-grey band like every other band, and its studio light is rebuilt from the band** (the client's #32, PROTECTED.md P1/P2; the operator's decision of 02.10.2026: „Adapt the studio light, do not keep the hero grey“). The old light — white fading to transparent white, 60 % × 50 % of the frame at 50 % / 62 % — was still a quarter strong at the frame's bottom edge and a sixth at its sides, so the frame cut it; on the neutral grey that cut was 1–3 levels and unseen, on the blue-grey 3–5 levels and a pale box above the caption (D-115 had kept the hero grey for it). Now `--c-studio-light` is the band lifted three quarters of the way to white (`color-mix` in OKLab — the same lift the white light gave the grey), and the pool is 50 % × 38 % at the same centre, fading on (1 − t²)² in eight stops, so it reaches nothing exactly at the bottom and side edges with no slope left to see. Measured on the built page: the step across the frame's edges is 0 at 1440 and 390 (it was 3–5 on blue-grey with the old light); a contrast-stretched pair shows the cut gone. Both hero components, both grey overrides removed; the P1 baselines renewed (`--update-snapshots=all` — the band change sits inside the screenshot tolerance); pictures in `docs/review/hero-band/`. | The operator chose the blue-grey hero; the edge came from the light's geometry, not the colour, so the fix is a light that ends inside its frame and is made of whatever band it stands on. — this commit |
| D-140 | 2026-10-02 | **A stopped renderer is back within about half a minute, and the pages are server-rendered again from the first one after it** (the operator's brief of 02.10.2026, §4: „kill SSR and prove an SSR page returns within a minute“). Measured on the preview first, release `e982af1a`, with the hPanel cron lines in: killed at 11:04:11 UTC, the watchdog restarted it at 11:05:03 (52 s — cron's minute is its floor), and the first server-rendered homepage came at 65 s, because `BoundedSsrGateway` rests 30 s after each failure and did not ask the new renderer until the rest ran out. Two changes: `deploy/ssr-watch.sh` looks twice a run (at once and 30 s later; `SSR_WATCH_AGAIN=0` looks once), so a stopped renderer is back within about half a minute; and the gateway's rest records when it began and ends as soon as the renderer's socket file is newer than that — the watchdog's restart creates it anew — so the first page after the restart is rendered on the server. A renderer that stalls without being restarted keeps its full rest. Worst case now ≈ 30 s + the start; to be measured on the next release. HOST-FINDINGS 10, GO-LIVE §6; a Pest test proves both sides of the socket rule. | The criterion is a page, not a process: the renderer was back inside the minute, the customer's page was not — the rest has to end when there is something to ask. — this commit |
| D-141 | 2026-10-02 | **No shared copy keeps an admin password that stood in the repository, and none gets one again** (the operator, 02.10.2026, „rotate the passwords immediately, not after“ — the repository had been public since 23.09.2026). Measured on the preview: ten accounts opened with a password from the repository — the three review accounts (`rimify-review-2026`, `admin@rimify.test` a Super-Admin) and the seven per-role development accounts AccessSeeder had made when the database was first seeded on 21.09.2026 (`rimify-dev-2026`, `super-admin@rimify.test` a Super-Admin; the sign-in asks for no second factor, AdminLoginController). All ten got new random passwords on 02.10.2026 (generated on the operator's machine, sent on the SSH session's stdin, written only into his `docs/review/ACCOUNTS.md`, which git now ignores), every session of them ended (`sessions_revoked_at`, remember tokens rotated), one Protokoll entry each (`admin.password_reset`). Two causes are closed: **ReviewAccountsSeeder**, run by every staging deploy, put the review password back each time — it now sets a password only when it makes an account, the known one only on a local or testing database, a random one elsewhere, and never resets one; **AccessSeeder** made the per-role accounts on every first seed, the live shop's included — it now makes them only on a local or testing database (the roles and the matrix everywhere). `rimify:admin-passwort <email>` sets a password from the operator's side (asked for, never an argument; 16 characters at least; never a known one) and ends the account's sessions; `rimify:preflight` names every account that may sign in and opens with a known password, NO-GO in production. The e2e specs and the admin sweep read the development password from `e2e/support.ts`, the preview's from the operator's file per account. The audit of the preview found no settings, storage, payment, role or user change by anyone from outside our addresses; `super-admin@rimify.test` was used from two German addresses on 01.–02.10. (a test wheel „Rimify R1“ created and published, an unreleased „Teilegutachten TEST123“) — probably the client, not provable. No S3 key and no payment key was ever on the preview. | A public password is a door, whoever was meant to use it; the rotation closes it, and the seeders that kept reopening it on every deploy — and would have opened it on the live shop — no longer can. — this commit |

The server decides it (`reviewer` in HandleInertiaRequests: the review on, an admin who may send a point — ReviewFeedbackPolicy — whose session still counts by EnsureAdminSessionValid's rules; not under /admin, not inside the Inhalte preview frame), so a visitor's page carries neither the prop nor the component's code (an async chunk under `v-if`). ShopFeedback.vue: bottom right, a light face lifted by its shadow (it reads on white, the blue-grey band and the dark footer), the bubble alone on a phone (44 × 44, same name), riding 12 px above whatever holds the bottom edge — the price bar, the action bar, the compare tray, measured because the tray on a narrow window wraps to two lines — absent while the cookie card is unanswered, the footer kept clear of it, the system's spinner while the page is drawn. The dialog is the admin's (`surface="shop"` with the shop's chips and notes), it answers a failed send itself in German with the sentence kept, and it closes when the page behind it changes. | D-145 | 2026-10-03 | **The renderer the watchdog starts no longer keeps the watchdog from running** (HOST-FINDINGS 11; found re-measuring D-140 on release `05fecd51`, as the operator's brief of 02.10.2026 §4 asked). hPanel runs each cron job under `flock -w 1`, holding the lock on a descriptor the job's children inherit; the renderer `ssr-watch.sh` started kept it, so while that renderer lived every later run gave up after a second — a renderer that hung would never have been replaced, and one that died came back at the next minute's first look, not within half a minute. `ssr-watch.sh` now starts the renderer in a subshell that closes every descriptor above 2 first (read from `/proc/$BASHPID/fd`), so the renderer holds only its log. Proven on the host under the same `flock -w 1` before the release. | The watchdog's two looks a minute only work if every run can start; nothing it starts may hold the lock its runs need. — this commit |

The eleven test orders so far were given back once the same way. | D-148 | 2026-10-03 | **Everyday pushes run the fast checks; the full matrix runs on `release`** (the operator, 03.10.2026, the repository going private: 2.000 Actions minutes a month). Measured before the split, from the last runs: a push to main cost 35–46 billed minutes — Pest on MariaDB 11.8 ≈ 19 (18,3 of them Pest itself), on MySQL 8.4 ≈ 13–23, the Deploy build 2–3; set-up under a minute a leg; no browser suite in CI. Now one reusable definition (`suite.yml`) serves two workflows: **Checks** on every push to main — Pint, Larastan, ESLint, Stylelint, vue-tsc, Vitest and the whole Pest suite on MariaDB (the host's engine) in one job, so one set-up and one rounding to the minute; **Release** on a push to `release` — the same on MariaDB plus Pest on MySQL 8.4. Pest runs `--parallel` (one process per core; the suite's parallel isolation exists since 01.10 — 2.320 tests green with 2 processes locally). A push of documentation alone (`docs/**`, `*.md`) runs neither Checks nor the Deploy build, and one of tests alone (`tests/**`, `e2e/**`) no Deploy build. The browser suite stays local: three clean full runs before a report. StrictModeTest now reads the release matrix. **Measured after the split** (270ec10d, GitHub's 4-core runners while the repository was still public): Checks 9,4 and 12,9 min in two runs (billed 10 and 13; Pest 6,5 instead of 18,3), Deploy 2,9 (3) — an everyday code push 13–16 billed minutes; Release 22 (MariaDB leg 14 with lint, types and Vitest, MySQL 8). A private repository on the free plan runs on 2 cores: about 15–20 an everyday push and 25 a release expected — 2.000 minutes ≈ 100–130 everyday code pushes a month, documentation pushes free. | The minutes go where a release is decided; an everyday push answers fast and cheap, a documentation push costs nothing. — this commit |

| D-150 | 2026-10-04 | **Testumgebung zurücksetzen is a restore, with three levels, a counted preview, the typed word, a 7-day undo and an audit entry first** (the reset brief §2–§4). Deviations from the brief, each the safer reading: the kept tables (team, roles, settings, Protokoll, reference data, invoice numbers) are never replaced at all rather than restored and re-applied — same outcome, nothing to lose on the way; the brief's `migrate` step became a refusal while a migration is pending, since the reset never changes the schema; „Alles“ resets pages, FAQ and menus to the delivered state, not to nothing — an empty content set would take the Impressum link off every page; the baseline holds the seven standard roles, not three, because the panel never deletes one; uploaded brand logos and the example drawings count as the shop's pictures. „Demodaten“ stays DemoPurge (refused as a whole). Ids are never given twice (AUTO_INCREMENT kept at its high-water), undo steps back through earlier resets in order, never in production (routes not registered, policy, service, preflight NO-GO). | One way in, one way back, nothing half; R-12's Protokoll is carried whole. R-04/R-12 speak of rows in a live database; on staging the reset replaces tables whose rows (test orders, cars) stay in the 7-day copy — CLAUDE.md does not say this yet, the operator's word is asked. — `3d454b04` |

| D-154 | 2026-10-05 | **Three guards around the reset, each proven by a test that fails without it** (the brief's §3; a script took each of the 11 out in turn and ran its test: 11 caught). 3.1 `ResetProbe`: the four triggers by name, an UPDATE and a DELETE of a real Protokoll entry and of a real Prüfnachweis refused with SQLSTATE 45000, and deleting the order line under a Prüfnachweis refused with 1451 — each check in its own rolled-back transaction (a throwaway order where the shop is empty), before every reset and undo (refused and recorded) and after every restore, undo and put-back (failed, put back, each check named in the Protokoll). 3.2 production refuses with a non-zero exit and identical table checksums, flag set or not; the flag alone, a test environment alone and the live database's row each refuse; the screen answers 404 — never 403, never a sign-in — wherever the reset is unavailable (`EnsureResetAvailable` at the front of the web group, as EnsureAdminPanelEnabled, since route middleware is sorted after Authorize). 3.3 the screen says how long the undo still works („noch 6 Tage und 23 Stunden“), the button goes with one sentence when it does not, and pruning a copy writes `system.reset_backup_removed`; a copy the disk has no room for is never begun (tables' data + moved files + 64 MB against `disk_free_space`), and a copy is written to `.part`, every write checked, closed, renamed, read back — on Hostinger PHP sees the 21 TB volume's 6,8 TiB free, not the account's quota, so the written-and-checked copy is the real guard there. | „Prove, not read“: a guard nobody has seen fail is a guess. — `b31898e2` |

| D-161 | 2026-10-05 | **The reset's baseline is tied to the schema** (the operator's brief of 05.10.2026, §2.1). `rimify:reset-baseline` records the migrations, the last one's name and batch, a schema fingerprint (`SchemaFingerprint`: tables, columns with type, nullability, auto-increment and generation, indexes, foreign keys with rules, triggers) and the app (commit, ReleaseSeed version). Before any restore `BaselineState` compares the baseline with the code's migration files, with the database's migrations and with its fingerprint, and refuses on any difference, naming both sides in German (non-zero exit, not one row changed — BaselineStateTest). The fingerprint is engine-neutral, measured on the same 80 migrations: the engines differ only in how they label an unsaid foreign-key rule (MySQL NO ACTION, MariaDB RESTRICT — 73 keys, identical in InnoDB) and in their ORDER BY collation, both normalised; the preview's database reads `b7ddada7`, the same as a fresh migrate on both engines. The deploy build rebuilds the baseline on MariaDB 11.8.9 whenever `--check` finds a migration not in it and ships it in the release (it cannot be built on the host: the account cannot create databases); `rimify:smoke` asserts it fits. | A restore never takes the database back in time while the code moves on. — `e4fc579d` |
| D-162 | 2026-10-05 | **A reset's safety copy is sealed, private and has an end** (the brief's §2.2). Measured first: over HTTPS the copy, its manifest, the log and `.env` answer 403 — the root `.htaccess` rewrites every request to `public/` — but that was one rule inside `<IfModule mod_rewrite.c>`. Now: copies are written AES-256-GCM in 1 MiB chunks under a per-copy key (HKDF of APP_KEY; openssl — the host's PHP 8.4.25 has no sodium), each chunk authenticated with its index and last-flag so a copy cut, altered or extended does not open; folders 0700, files 0600; a copy from before is sealed by the next prune and compared byte for byte before its plain file goes; `storage/app/private`, `storage/framework` and `storage/logs` refuse every request on their own, and the root serves nothing at all without mod_rewrite; prune runs hourly, so a copy goes within the hour after its seven days; `rimify:smoke` asks the real server for the newest copy, the log and two canaries and fails on anything but 403/404. The command prints the copy's own size and read-back result instead of a free-space figure the host does not mean. The host step's own `~/backups` (19 dumps, 6 code archives, 6 `.env` copies, 834 MB on 05.10.2026) get the same treatment from `remote.sh`: the newest five sets always, older sets after 14 days — the first would go on 13.10.2026. | Customer data at rest has an end date and a lock, and the lock is proven from outside. — `e4fc579d` |

| D-165 | 2026-10-05 | **Archivo's fallback faces are computed from the font file per weight and width; measures in characters use Archivo's own; reduced motion switches transitions off; the admin rail is decided in the first frame** (the operator's brief of 05.10.2026, §4). fontaine wrote one face per fallback, for weight 400 at 100 %, all three under one family with identical descriptors — only the last, Arial, ever counted, Android (no Arial) fell to unadjusted Roboto, and text at 110/112 % and weight 700 (every heading) was set up to 17 % narrower than Archivo. `scripts/fonts/fallbacks.mjs` reads the woff2 itself — its metric tables as a TrueType of their own, `hmtx` plus the `HVAR` delta per instance (fontkitten cannot take an instance of a WOFF2) — and writes 40 faces: Arial (with Liberation Sans) and Roboto as two families, four weight × five width bands; it reproduces capsize's published Archivo widths at 100 % (440/447/456/472). `fonts.test.ts` fails when the font changes without regeneration, when the CSS is edited by hand, when a weight or width in use has no face, when the `--ch-*` tokens differ from the font's „0", and when a new `ch` measure appears (146 left; a box in `ch` changes width on the swap). fontaine out, fontkitten in (dev). `e2e/cls-budget.spec.ts` (font held back 1,5 s; 390, 768, 1024, 1280, 1440 px; both motion settings; shop, empty shop, admin) found and this fixes: the hero's headline and the cookie sheet in `ch` (0,033 phone, 0,027 at 768); the reduced-motion rule giving every element a 0,01 ms transition, which started one wherever a style settled (0,054 at 1024 — transitions are now 0 s there); the admin rail collapsed by a script at the end of the body that ran after the first paint (0,19 at 1024 — now from the head, in the first frame that shows the shell). After: ≤ 0,012 on every page and width measured. | The swap holds the room the font will take, at every weight and width the shop uses. — this commit |

| D-176 | 2026-10-06 | **D-175 withdrawn: behind Hostinger's CDN the document's encoding is the CDN's** (measured on the preview after the deploy of `008959e3`). At the origin the middleware did what it said — a browser-like request got the app's gzip, 45,0 KB — but the CDN negotiates the encoding itself and hands every browser that takes brotli its own brotli, 58,4 KB, whatever the app sends; only a client without brotli ever saw the gzip. Reverted. What stays measured: in 15 of 16 samples the origin answered within 0,29–0,53 s (the CDN's upstream time); the large swings in the phone's LCP from here (medians 2,08 s and 2,48 s in two sessions, single loads up to 5,9 s) sit on the network between this machine and the CDN's edge — the same build behind a local compressing proxy paints the phone's headline at 0,96 s. | D-182 | 2026-10-06 | **The shop's mail goes through Amazon SES in `eu-north-1`, the bucket's account and region, with a key of its own entered in Einstellungen › E-Mail; until it is cleared, mail stays in the log and `rimify:go-live-check` says „fehlt“** (the brief „AFTER CI“, §3). The screen is built as Medien und Speicher: Super-Admin only, password asked again, keys write-only and encrypted, every save tested against SES (`GetAccount`, `GetEmailIdentity`), a refused key kept as pending. The shop switches its default mailer to SES only when the last test clears it — the key works, sending is enabled, the account has **production access** (in the sandbox a customer's confirmation would be refused), the sender's domain is verified — and never because a key was merely saved. Two go-live lines: „E-Mail-Versand über Amazon SES“ (what is open, in order) and „Absenderdomain: SPF, DKIM und DMARC“, which looks every record up as the world sees it and names each missing one with type, name and value — SPF on a MAIL FROM subdomain (`bounce.<domain>`, so the client's own mailbox records stay untouched), the three DKIM CNAMEs SES names, DMARC at `p=none` first. Bounces and complaints arrive by SNS at `POST /webhooks/ses`, accepted only when SNS signed them (version 1 and 2, the certificate fetched from an `sns.<region>.amazonaws.com` address over https only) and only from the topic entered on the screen; a permanent bounce or a complaint blocks each address once — the SNS message id makes a repeat a no-op — and says so in the Protokoll; nothing is sent to a blocked address (`DropSuppressedRecipients`), and the screen releases one. `rimify:mail-test --anhaenge` is the deliverability test: the confirmation's layout with two PDFs, for Gmail, Outlook and GMX; `docs/ops/MAIL-SES.md` is the operator's setup and the table its result goes in. | Mail is how a customer receives the contract (§ 312f BGB): it must leave from an EU region, reach the inbox, and never go to an address that bounced or complained — and until it can, the shop must say so rather than look finished. No package added: the AWS SDK is already there for the bucket. — this commit |
| D-183 | 2026-10-06 | **`docs/legal/VERARBEITUNG.md` is generated from the installation's configuration (`rimify:verarbeitung`), and `npm run qa` fails on any request a page makes to a third-party host** (the brief „AFTER CI“, §6). The record names each provider — Hostinger (web server, database, CDN), AWS S3 (the bucket), Amazon SES, Stripe, the shop's own mailbox (its provider read off the domain's MX) — with company and address, place, purpose, data, legal basis and agreement, and **the state each is in there**: the server's country from its name, the bucket in force and its region, SES sending or not, Stripe live, test, sandbox or off. The retention table reads the constants and settings that enforce it (`ContactMessage::KEEP_MONTHS`, `OrderPayments::HOURS_TO_PAY`, `TestEnvironment::KEEP_DAYS` — the 7-day reset copy —, the log's `max_files`, the session's lifetime, the link and invitation lifetimes, the cookie list), and says plainly where the code enforces no end: expired unpaid orders, invoices and Prüfnachweise past their duty, the renderer's log — each listed under „Offen für die Kanzlei“. It is run where the configuration is real (on the server) and the file brought back. `scripts/qa/third-party.mjs` (G8, first in `npm run qa`) opens every route at 390 and 1440, records every request of every frame, and fails on any cross-origin one that is not the bucket the page's own policy names, or Stripe on the checkout (its script, its frames, what its frames ask for); it proves itself first — a page asking example.org for an image must fail it, a clean one must pass. Measured 06.10.2026: no third-party request on the local e2e shop, nor on the preview behind Hostinger's CDN. | The processing record must be the shop as it runs, not as someone remembered it; and „no third party at runtime“ is a promise the Datenschutzerklärung will make — a gate that fails the build is what keeps it true. — this commit |

| D-203 | 2026-10-13 | **The contact form's three seconds are three seconds: the ticket carries the time it was handed out to the millisecond.** In whole seconds (`35babb2b`) a form handed out at x,9 s and sent at x+3,0 s counted as three seconds old after 2,1 — the check was „two to three, depending on where in the second the page came“. | D-204 | 2026-10-13 | **A test run is refused on a `tests/env.php` that is not the committed one** (the operator's brief „UNBLOCK, PATCH“, §2.2). Its fingerprint — SHA-256, line ends as LF — stands in `tests/env.sha256`; `PinnedEnvironment::apply()` compares before anything else and names both fingerprints, the way back (`git checkout -- tests/env.php`) and the way forward (write the new fingerprint, if the change is meant). Only the look-back, which flips pins by design, runs on a changed list, and says so with `TEST_LOOKBACK=1` — a name the application never reads. Why: the look-back flips a pin, runs, and puts it back; on 06.10. the machine went down in between, and its worktree was left with `RIMIFY_FEATURE_PARTNERS => 'true'` for a week. The next ordinary run there would have reported on that environment. Proven on the real file: flipped, the run exits 2 naming the fingerprint the poisoned file had; with the switch it runs; restored, it is clean. | The list is the one file whose job is to make the suite honest; nothing checked the list. — this commit |
| D-205 | 2026-10-13 | **Every mail prints what it is given literally: a visitor's words and a Gutachten's sentences are text, never Markdown** (`App\Mail\LiteralMarkdown`, `App\Support\MailText`). A mail template is Markdown, and what `{{ }}` prints into it was read as Markdown too — the framework escapes it for HTML only. Proven before fixing: a contact message „[Hier klicken](https://…)“ became a link, and „![](https://…)“ a remote image, in the copy the shop sends **to whatever address the visitor typed**; and of the 177 Auflagen in the three transcribed Gutachten one was changed in the order mail — „… ab einschließlich EG-Typgenehmigungs-Nr. e1*93/81*…“ lost its stars to italics. The rule sits in the renderer, not in the templates, so there is no mail and no variable it can be forgotten for: each sign Markdown reads as syntax is written as a numeric character reference, which CommonMark takes as the character and never as syntax (spec 6.2) and the plain-text part decodes — a backslash would have stood in the plain text. Tests: the parser's own output, read as text, equals what went in, for sixteen kinds of syntax and for all 177 Auflagen; both contact mails. Found while reading what `league/commonmark`'s advisories of 30.09. touch (bumped 2.10.1 → 2.10.3): the high one — quadratic time in the table extension's block-start scan — is in the path a visitor's message takes, bounded by the form's 5.000 characters and five messages a minute; the medium one concerns an extension the mail renderer does not load. `source-map-js` 1.2.1 → 1.2.2 (build tooling only); with it `concurrently` (critical, via `shell-quote`) and `sharp`, whose fixes are not major. Left: 18 high advisories in development tools only (`lighthouse`/`puppeteer`, `stylelint`, `@gltf-transform/cli`) whose offered „fix“ is a major downgrade — nothing of them ships. | The same shape as D-199, one layer down: words printed through something that may change them. | D-207 | 2026-10-13 | **A release can be built by hand while GitHub Actions is down, and is proven against the last CI-built one** (`scripts/deploy/release-ohne-ci.ps1`, `docs/ops/DEPLOY-OHNE-CI.md`). | D-214 | 2026-10-14 | **A wheel its Gutachten approves, with a tyre size nobody could confirm, is said as that — the operator's decision on finding 103.** Seen on the client's own wheel and car (BBS LeMans × BMW 3er Coupe 346C): the panel was headed „Keine Freigabe hinterlegt für …“ over a row „Felge: Freigegeben mit Auflagen“, and the size was struck through with „… liegt uns … keine Freigabe vor“. Now: the headline leads with the true half, „Felge freigegeben mit Auflagen · Reifengröße noch nicht bestätigt“; a sentence under it says which half is which; the size is marked grey and dashed, never struck through, and can be chosen, with the legend „Grau markierte Größen können wir für dein Fahrzeug noch nicht bestätigen. Das heißt nicht, dass sie unzulässig sind, sondern dass uns die Unterlagen dazu fehlen.“; and there is a way on — „Wenn du deine Wunschreifen nennst, prüfen wir sie vorab von Hand.“ with a link to the contact form, the wheel and size as its subject, the page as where it was written from, the car ticked. `tyreUnconfirmed()` is the one definition of the state (wheel half approved, tyre half UNKNOWN, no fact of the car missing); a size with nothing on record at all stays struck through and „Keine Freigabe hinterlegt“. Whether the wheel is *sold* in this state is unchanged (R-03): this Gutachten approves the wheel only „in Verbindung mit“ four named tyre combinations (§2 of Anlage BMW15 to 18 10 08 0716), so nothing is sold until the tyre is confirmed. Two of the operator's sentences are worded on record instead of as written — „… ist zu diesem Gutachten keine Tragfähigkeitstabelle … hinterlegt“ and „Sobald dazu eine Tragfähigkeitstabelle hinterlegt ist …“ rather than „liegt uns die Tabelle noch nicht vor“ / „Sobald uns die Tabelle vorliegt“ — because this very document prints no such table („Der erforderliche Geschwindigkeits-/Lastindex ist den Fahrzeugpapieren zu entnehmen“), and R-17, decided in the same message, forbids promising one. | Unverified is neither forbidden nor unavailable, and the difference is the product. The first page the client opens is his own wheel on his own car. — this commit |
| D-215 | 2026-10-14 | **„Zu diesem Gutachten ist keine Tragfähigkeitstabelle für hohe Geschwindigkeit hinterlegt.“ — finding 104, approved as proposed.** It replaces „Das Gutachten druckt keine Tabelle zur Tragfähigkeit bei hoher Geschwindigkeit.“ in the fitment engine (`FitmentResolver`) and in the four tests that hold the sentence. The shop knows what is on record with it; it does not know what a PDF prints, of which it may hold a part. | A claim about a legal document we may have entered only partly is a claim we cannot back; if the table is on a page we never received, we told a customer something untrue about it. — 82acd1fa |
| D-216 | 2026-10-14 | **R-17, „Say only what is on record“, is in the constitution — and with it a list, a test and eleven sentences.** The rule is the operator's, word for word (CLAUDE.md §3). `Tests\Support\OnRecord` finds every sentence in `app`, `resources/js`, `resources/views` and `lang` that names a Gutachten, an ABE, a Nachtrag, a manufacturer or a document and denies or restricts something in the same breath; `tests/Support/on-record-ledger.php` holds the verdict on each (71: 18 backed by a named field, 40 about the shop itself, 2 not sentences, 11 open with their finding), and `tests/Unit/OnRecordTest.php` fails on a sentence that is not listed and on an entry no longer written. `docs/review/ON-RECORD.md` is the list as a table. **Reworded, no verdict changed:** nine sentences of the engine that said what a document does not contain now say what is not on record with us („… nennt das Gutachten keine Reifengröße“ → „… ist bei uns keine Reifengröße hinterlegt“; before and after in ON-RECORD.md), and the bare „Für dieses Fahrzeug können wir die Freigabe nicht bestätigen.“ — one sentence for a removed vehicle and for missing reference tables — is two sentences that each name the cause; a test holds that no two reasons share a sentence. **Not changed, because they change what a customer is told:** the two ledes over the document's wording (finding 118) and the answer „nicht zulässig“ for a car the recorded rows do not name (finding 119). The admin's seven sentences of the same shape are recorded, not changed (ADMIN-GEFUNDEN 505). | Three findings of one family in three days; a rule nobody can test is a rule the client finds a fourth time. — 22bc9042 |
| D-217 | 2026-10-14 | **The go-live check counts the pairs that stand on „Felge freigegeben · Reifengröße noch nicht bestätigt“ — by asking the engine, and never while a page waits.** New check `reifenbestaetigung` in „Recht am Rad“ (so it is a line of `rimify:go-live-check` and of WAS-FEHLT.md): „2 von 3 Kombinationen aus Fahrzeug und Felge stehen auf … Zum Beispiel: …“, the example pairs named, pairs from born-demo documents counted apart and not held against the shop. `FitmentVerdict::tyreUnconfirmed()` is the state, once; `TyreConfirmations` resolves every pair a released, valid Gutachten names. That takes about ten milliseconds a pair — 26 s for the dev shop's 2,475 — so the command and an hourly schedule count and keep the result for a day; System › Was fehlt reads what is kept, counts itself only up to 200 pairs, and otherwise says „Noch nicht gezählt“. Without the engine's reference tables it reads „nicht prüfbar“ instead of failing the report. | A count that guesses with a query would be the fourth sentence of the family R-17 is about; a count that makes an admin page wait half a minute would be found by the client. Read on the preview with the engine deployed there today: 3 pairs, 2 open — both finishes of his BBS LeMans on his 346C. — ddc69f92 |
| D-218 | 2026-10-14 | **Finding 102 was a security bug (S1), and is closed as one: the shop no longer writes to an address a stranger typed.** What it was: the contact form mailed „a copy“ of whatever a visitor wrote to whatever address the visitor entered, and read the words as Markdown — so anyone could have the shop send a link and a remote image of their choosing, under the shop's name, to any inbox (a phishing relay and a tracking pixel in one). D-205 made the words literal. This closes the rest. **(1) Did such a mail ever leave?** No. The preview's mailer is `log`; no SMTP or SES is set in its `.env` or in Einstellungen › E-Mail (no row); `contact_messages` holds 0 rows — the form has never stored a message there; production does not exist yet. Read on the host, 14.10.2026. **(2) The copy** goes only to a confirmed address: a signed-in customer's own (`email_verified_at`, set when the account is opened through the mailed link), and only where it is the address typed (`ContactRequest::copyAddress()`). A guest gets the on-page confirmation and no mail; the page promises a copy only where one is sent. **(3) No mail fetches anything from another host:** the templates carry no image and no foreign address (tested over every template, and that every Mailable is built from one); the layout's header is ours — the framework's prints a logo from laravel.com whenever `APP_NAME` is „Laravel“; and `StripRemoteMailContent` takes out of every outgoing mail what a mail program would fetch elsewhere (`img`, `link`, `iframe`, `background`, CSS `url()` and `@import`), sends the mail without it and logs it. Links stay: a person follows a link, a program fetches an image. **(4) Limits:** five messages a minute per visitor (as before), thirty an hour per network address (was 3,000), three an hour and ten a day per e-mail address named — counted by a hash, so the cache holds no address; the body stays capped at 5,000 characters; name and subject are one line each (both end in mail headers). **(5) Siblings, read one by one:** every mail prints typed text literally (D-205, all Mailables share `LiteralMarkdown`); the invoice PDF and every Vue page escape (`{{ }}`; the only `v-html` are our own icons and artwork; no `{!! !!}` over data); the Protokoll and Verkauf › Nachrichten print text as text; one link did not — an order's „E-Mail“ in the admin wrote the customer's address raw into a `mailto:`, where `x?bcc=…@…` would have chosen a recipient: now encoded (`mailtoHref`, ADMIN-GEFUNDEN 507). | A form that mails strangers is abused within days of going live, and the mail carries the shop's name and domain reputation. The limit per e-mail address means a third party can hold one address off the form for an hour by naming it three times — accepted: the alternative is that they can write to it without limit. — 49128a5c |
| D-219 | 2026-10-14 | **The image rule: no picture on the first screen loads lazily, and none outside it is preloaded — at 1440 × 900 and at 390 × 844, by test.** The operator's rule, word for word (brief of 14.10.2026, §5); `e2e/image-loading.spec.ts` holds it over the homepage, the listing (plain and filtered), a product page, compare, the RIMIFY-Check, the vehicle picker, the basket and contact. „On the first screen“ is a single pixel of the picture in view; „preloaded“ is a `<link rel="preload">` or `fetchpriority="high"`. **What it found and what changed:** (1) finding 110 was not what its row said — the listing's first-row photographs were never lazy; every tile's *second* photograph, the one that fades in under a mouse, sat invisible on top of the first row and was fetched with the page. It is now fetched when a mouse first comes over the tile, never for a finger. (2) The product page's thumbnails beside the photograph were lazy on the first screen: now loaded at once, after the photograph. (3) The listing loads its first screen at once — eight tiles on a desktop, four on a phone, the server knows which — and asks for the first bytes for one picture only, the first tile's; before, four did. `Picture` has a fourth way of loading for this, `prompt`. (4) The phone hero's two wheels were lazy on the strength of „just below the first screen“ (05.10.2026, top at 861 px); the frame's top is at 838 px now and 23 px of it show while it rolls in. They are loaded at once at low priority. **Measured before changing it** (Slow 4G, CPU × 4, seven interleaved loads, the document rewritten for the second variant): FCP 1,94 s against 1,96 s, LCP 3,39 s against 3,12 s — the spread of either is wider than the difference — and the wheel is there 1,9 s sooner. With it: the vehicle picker's two headings are second-level (finding 111), the listing has a second-level heading „Ergebnisse“ over its tiles on every width (112), and the promises section is called „Was wir zusagen“ on the phone as on the desktop and in the design document (113). | A rule that held only at one viewport by 6 px was not a rule; and the phone decision of 05.10. was right for the layout it measured, which the page no longer has. — 6be30b0d |
| D-220 | 2026-10-14 | **The sweep's second pass, fixed where it was cheap and safe: a way on from a refused wheel, the header at 1024 px, the car named at the checkout, „Summe ohne Versand“, the ledes over the Gutachten's words, and eleven wordings.** (1) Finding 117: under „Nicht freigegeben“, „Keine Freigabe hinterlegt“ and „Anlage liegt noch nicht vor“ the panel now offers „Felgen für deinen {car} anzeigen“ — not where the answer waits for a fact the customer gives or only the tyre size is open, which have their own way on. (2) 123: from 1024 to 1279 px the header was 21 px too wide; the basket keeps icon and count there and the bar's links stay on one line (`e2e/widths.spec.ts`, ten sizes from 320 to 1920 and short windows). (5) 126: „E-Mail info@…:“ — one colon in the model form's address line. (6) 118, **R-17 applied, yours to veto:** „Wörtlich aus dem Gutachten.“ → „Der Wortlaut des Gutachtens, wie er bei uns hinterlegt ist.“; „So steht es im Gutachten – ohne unsere Gliederung und ohne Auslassungen.“ → „Der hinterlegte Wortlaut des Gutachtens, ohne unsere Gliederung.“ (7) 127, before → after: „nur, dass wir es nicht belegen können“ → „nur, dass wir nicht belegen können, dass sie zulässig ist/sind“ (RIMIFY-Check, twice); „Dann kann eine Tachoangleichung nötig werden – das steht im Gutachten.“ → „Zeigt der Tacho dadurch weniger an, als du fährst, kann eine Tachoangleichung nötig sein – prüfe dazu die Auflagen des Gutachtens.“; „Die neue Felge steht rechnerisch … weiter außen/innen“ → „Die Außenkante der neuen Felge steht …“; „Ob Rad und Reifen frei laufen … die Bedingungen nennt …“ → „Ob Felge und Reifen frei laufen … die Auflagen dazu nennt …“; „Zulässig unter den Bedingungen aus dem Gutachten“ → „… unter den Auflagen des Gutachtens“; empty checkout „Lege zuerst eine Felge in den Warenkorb …“ → „Wähle dein Fahrzeug und lege eine Felge in den Warenkorb …“; empty account „Unter dieser Adresse gibt es noch keine Bestellung.“ → „Mit dieser E-Mail-Adresse hast du noch nichts bestellt. Fahrzeug wählen und passende Felgen finden.“ (with the link); „Auto wählen“ → „Fahrzeug wählen“ (vehicle picker); the 404's „oder diese Felge steht nicht mehr im Sortiment“ → „… verschoben oder entfernt, etwa weil eine Felge nicht mehr im Sortiment ist“; contact help „du findest sie im Fahrzeugschein“ → „sie stehen in deiner Zulassungsbescheinigung Teil I, Felder 2.1 und 2.2“; the legal placeholder „… die Kanzlei des Shopbetreibers; sobald er vorliegt, steht er im Shop unter Rechtliches.“ → „Diesen Text erstellt gerade unsere Kanzlei. Sobald er vorliegt, steht er hier.“; the cookie table's „Oder du löschst …“ → „Du kannst die Cookies dieser Seite auch in deinem Browser löschen.“ The copy review's other twenty points are listed in EIGENE-FUNDE 128. | Each is reversible in minutes; none changes a verdict. The ledes are the one that tells a customer something about the conditions — reworded towards less, and named first in the report. — this commit |
