| D-226 | 2026-10-14 | **The bucket check asks the CORS question the way a browser asks it, where the key may not read the rule (ADMIN-GEFUNDEN 502, brief of 14.10.2026 §1).** The smoke's „Speicher-Präfixe“ row was red on „Keine CORS-Regel“ because the bucket's key lacks `s3:GetBucketCORS` — a rule it cannot see is not a missing rule. The Gutachten upload is an XHR POST with progress, so every browser sends a preflight first; row 7 now sends that OPTIONS request from the shop's origin, for PUT and for POST, and passes only on 200 with our origin allowed. On the preview's bucket today: 200 for `https://rimify.skillleo.com` (PUT, POST allowed), 403 for any other origin — the rule was there all along. „Keine CORS-Regel“ is said only where AWS says exactly that (`NoSuchCORSConfiguration`). | `S3AuditTest` |
| D-227 | 2026-10-14 | **A Gutachten is incomplete until someone asserts it is complete (EIGENE-FUNDE 119, your brief of 14.10.2026 §2).** Until now a document counted as complete unless someone filled the optional „fehlende Anlagen“, and a car its rows did not name was answered „nicht zulässig“ — wrong in the direction that costs most (ABE 53841: we hold Anlage 16 of 16; fifteen Anlagen of cars would each have been refused, falsely), and the go-live check read the same empty field as „Anlagen vollständig · ok“. Now: the release asks „Sind alle Anlagen und alle Fahrzeuge dieses Gutachtens erfasst?“ with three answers and no default — Ja, alle / Teilweise (what is missing, required) / Weiß ich nicht; a Teilegutachten starts at „Teilweise“, as its type says. The answer is recorded with who, when and the revision (`approval_documents.completeness*`) and in the Protokoll. The engine refuses a car the rows do not name only on „Ja, alle“; otherwise it answers „Noch nicht bestätigt“ — with the missing Anlagen named: „Für dieses Fahrzeug liegt uns die passende Anlage des Gutachtens noch nicht vor – sag uns dein Auto, und wir holen sie.“ (your words), without: „Dein Fahrzeug ist zu diesem Gutachten bei uns noch nicht erfasst. Sag uns dein Auto, und wir prüfen, ob das Gutachten es nennt.“ Every document released before today has no answer and reads as partial; a released document is answered on its page („Jetzt beantworten“). The go-live check passes „Anlagen und Fahrzeuge vollständig erfasst“ only on an explicit yes for every released document and names each other one; while any released document is unanswered, no real payment is taken (`liveRefusal`). | `FitmentResolverTest`, `GoLiveChecksTest`, `ReleaseOnInstructionTest` |
| D-228 | 2026-10-14 | **A release counts what the PDF prints against what was entered, and refuses on any difference (your brief of 14.10.2026 §4 — the first permissive error, S1).** The client's Teilegutachten 1810080716 was released with one Auflage of the six its Verwendungsbereich prints for 346C, one tyre size of three (four combinations), no Abnahme and not the footnote „nur für Fahrzeuge mit maximaler Achslast 1160 kg“; his „ABE 53841“ (the Nachtrag 366-0350-21-WIRD/N1) with none of the 27 codes it prints for 346C — among them 11A (Abnahme), six Karosserie codes, 5FE/5GA (axle-load limits) — and a kW band from 70 where the document starts at 77. Every earlier gap made us too cautious; these two make us too generous. Now the release asks, read off the PDF for the vehicles entered: how many Auflagen codes, how many tyre sizes, the axle-load limit if one is named, and whether an Abnahme is asked for every combination, for some, or none. The release counts the same in the rows and refuses on any difference, in sentences („Auflagen-Codes für die erfassten Fahrzeuge laut Verwendungsbereich: 6, erfasst: 1. …“). An axle-load limit reaches the shop as the wheel's tested load (half the axle per wheel, the engine's existing check), so a named limit without a tested load on every version of the wheel refuses the release. The same questions on the command line (`rimify:gutachten-freigeben --erfasst --codes --groessen --achslast --abnahme`). Both of the client's released entries were taken back with `rimify:gutachten-zuruecknehmen` (D-225) until they are complete. | `ReleaseOnInstructionTest` |
| D-229 | 2026-10-14 | **A Gutachten's PDF is found where it is, when it is read (EIGENE-FUNDE 105's cause, ADMIN-GEFUNDEN 501, your brief §6).** Switching storage to the bucket moved no file, and a link built for wherever storage points now answered 403 for both of the client's documents. `DocumentFiles` looks on the disk in force, then on the server's disk; a link is only ever built to where the file is, and a file found nowhere is said to be missing (the admin's record, the log, the go-live check) — never handed to a customer as a link that fails. `rimify:pdf-migrate` copies the stragglers into the disk in force, reads each back against the SHA-256 on record, leaves the original, and reports every document (`--nur-pruefen` to look only). The go-live check „Jedes freigegebene Gutachten-PDF lässt sich öffnen“ fetches every released document's PDF at the address the shop links and fails unless it answers with a PDF. | `DocumentFilesTest` |

Three tests lied in one week (the operator's count): the widths test timing out with nothing wrong, #23 whose setup pressed the sign-in form before the page had woken, and 508 — resolved, not an app fault: „Verwerfen“ asked for the view as saved, and the timeout's afterEach opened the bare list while the check still polled. The harness acting on the page out of step with what the test was looking at; for teardowns the rule ends it. **Found: twelve teardowns** that drove or ran code in the page (seven `goto` + `page.evaluate` for the saved views, five `page.evaluate` for the rail) **and four cleanups inside test bodies** that clicked or navigated (K2-01, K2-02, UEB-04, #23 on the preview). All reset over the context's own requests now (`e2e/teardown.ts`: `send`, `get`, `propsOf` — the session's cookies and XSRF header, the page's props read from its HTML); the browser side is thrown away by Playwright after every test. #23 writes on the preview only where a stopped run left the level otherwise, so the Protokoll gains no fourth line. `E2eTeardownTest` holds the three suites to it: no `afterEach`/`afterAll` takes the page or reaches one through the context, no fixture drives it after the test (`unrouteAll` and `isClosed` excepted). The three-minute budget that proved the cause is taken out again: a slow run fails as slow, on its own step. | A fixture that drives the browser after the body has finished can always race a locator that has not given up yet, however generous the timeout. — this commit |
| D-264 | 2026-10-17 | **The bucket check names the permission its key lacks, and says „keine Regel“ only where AWS does (ADMIN-GEFUNDEN 502, its second half).** D-226 fixed the CORS row; five more still read a refusal as an answer — the bucket list, region, versioning and encryption said „Nicht abfragbar“, and the lifecycle row said „Keine Regel“ for any failure, so on a key that may not read the setting a rule nobody could see was reported missing. Where AWS refuses the key (AccessDenied, 403) each row now says „Nicht lesbar – dem Schlüssel fehlt die Berechtigung …“ (s3:ListAllMyBuckets, s3:GetBucketLocation, s3:GetBucketVersioning, s3:GetLifecycleConfiguration, s3:GetEncryptionConfiguration) with AWS's own answer; „Keine Regel räumt …“ and „Keine Standardverschlüsselung eingestellt“ only on AWS's own NoSuch… answers; any other failure as what it is, „Abfrage fehlgeschlagen: …“ (the copy editor: one failed query is not a lasting state). The CORS row names s3:GetBucketCORS the same way. No row's status changed. Records corrected beside it: ADMIN-GEFUNDEN 501 and 505 still read „open“ for fixes made on 14.10. (D-229, D-236). | R-17's shape outside the shop: say what is unknown, why, and what would make it known. — this commit |
| D-265 | 2026-10-17 | **A Gutachten's PDF is known by its file: a real document's file is not entered a second time under another name, and every Gutachten page says where else its file lies (ADMIN-GEFUNDEN 503).** On the client's preview the Nachtrag 366-0350-21-WIRD/N1 had been entered as „ABE, KBA 53841“, and nothing noticed the file (D-210). Every upload was already hashed (`pdf_sha256`); now, once the upload is confirmed and before any row is written, `SamePdf::twins()` looks for that hash among the other documents outside the document's own revision line — a new Fassung shares its predecessor's file by design („Neue Fassung anlegen“, „Ersetzt eine frühere Fassung“). A real twin refuses the upload, names it and its state — „Diese PDF-Datei liegt schon bei „Nachtrag 366-0350-21-WIRD/N1“ (Entwurf). Ein Gutachten wird nur einmal angelegt – öffne es dort, oder lad die richtige Datei hoch.“ — and the confirmed copy is removed again; the same when a draft's file is swapped. An example document's file never stands in a real one's way: by the operator's yes the acceptance set shares a file with the client's own document (D-221), so a demo twin is let through and both pages say „Dieselbe Datei liegt auch bei …“, linked. On the preview his „ABE 53841“ will name the set's Nachtrag beside its PDF — the admin only; nothing a customer reads changes. The seeder's half, comparing hashes before it writes, came with D-221. | A number is typed; a file is what it is. A refused upload costs a minute; a document under the wrong name is a wrong answer waiting in the shop. — this commit |
| D-266 | 2026-10-17 | **An admin test signs in by request and opens what it tests: the sign-in form and the Übersicht are no longer the first half of every test's minute.** Release 9's full run beside another project's browser suite: 444 green, 9 red, every red on an admin page — six at the one-minute timeout, three on a step's own wait (RAIL-02's rig: the three keys landed before the first save left; SV-05c: the undo toast after 10 s; the sign-out after 10 s). Their traces show admin requests at 5–12 s against 0,7–1,7 s on a dedicated server, and 15–35 s of each minute spent on the form's page, the POST and the Übersicht it lands on before the test began. `signIn` (faq-support, re-exported by every admin support file) now asks the form's address as Inertia asks — a 409 that draws nothing but sets the session's cookies — and posts the sign-in over the context's own request; the page is left where it is and the test opens what it tests (89 of 117 callers did so on the next line; the bell tests now open the Übersicht themselves). A fresh session per test, as before — the admin keeps a sudo moment and an intended address in the session, so nothing is shared between tests. The form stays typed into where it is the subject (the role reviews, the admin sweeps). The Übersicht's width test, nine page loads in one minute, is three tests of three. No budget raised: a step that waits on a slow server still fails on its own step. The first re-run found two helpers that wrote from inside the page and so needed one open: the saved views' `send` (it read `document.cookie` of a blank page — SV-05b and c went red at once) and an in-body rail reset in admin-rail-scrollbars (D-263's class, which its sweep had missed, and which ran only when the test passed); both go by request now, the reset as an `afterEach`. The Übersicht's „one Zeitraum“ test put its 30 days back by navigating at the end of its body — the same class, and a red test left the account on 7 days for every test after it; an `afterEach` puts it back by request now, only after a test that changed it. Two more shapes of the same mistake, fixed where the runs found them: a rig that read the server's first ANSWER where it meant the first REQUEST (RAIL-02 — under load the answers came back out of order), or looked for the tab-closing beacon's width with a whole Übersicht per look (now: the tab really closed, then the account over a light page until the beacon has landed); and a page assertion whose 10 s silently also had to hold two server round trips (saving a view, signing out) — the server's answer is now a step of its own, then the page. | A test's minute is for what it tests. — `27d88995`, and the commits after it |
| D-267 | 2026-10-17 | **The e2e server hands out the files under public/ itself; PHP gets only the app's own requests.** Release 10's first full run with D-266: 451 green, 4 red — CHECK-08's refusal, GUTACHTEN-08c's confirm dialog, K2-01's new brand and PAY-01's payment, each server work inside a page wait. PHP's built-in server serves a request for an existing file in the worker it reached: an admin page's thirty scripts held all four workers of `scripts/e2e/serve.mjs`, and the page's own save or sign-out queued behind them — on a loaded machine past the wait. In production LiteSpeed and the CDN serve these files and PHP never sees them; the e2e proxy now does the same: any existing file under public/ (the files server.php used to hand over), except a .php file, a dotfile or a directory, which still go to PHP; nothing outside public/ (traversal answered 404 by the app). Checked on a spare port: scripts, fonts and the manifest from the proxy with their types in 0,1–0,3 s; /up, the sign-in page and a missing file from PHP as before. The full run now writes the machine's CPU every 15 s beside its logs, so a red can be set against the load it ran under. | Production's shape for the test servers too: PHP for the app, not for files. — this commit |
| D-268 | 2026-10-17 | **The acceptance set's Gutachten carry their own example copies — the document byte for byte, „BEISPIEL“ across every page and a band along the top that says what the file is — and never a real document's file (the operator's brief of 17.10.2026 §6).** Checked first, read-only on the preview: the client's #18 „ABE 53841“ and the set's #20 Nachtrag were the same bytes but never the same file — his `gutachten/01m4ke425kh0sathp4g07bbw1w.pdf`, the set's copy `gutachten/abnahme-nachtrag-366-0350-21.pdf` — and the demo purge removes only `abnahme-` files no row names (`DemoCatalogue::forgetDrawings`); the reset's demo level only copies the drawings. No S1: the purge cannot reach his file. Untangled anyway, as decision 6 asks: D-239 had marked the document wherever the shop shows it, but the file itself carried no mark — the stack had no tool to stamp a PDF. Now `ExamplePdf` stamps it with FPDI's parser (`setasign/fpdi`, MIT) by an incremental update: the original's bytes stay as they are, an appended section lays the mark over each page (see-through, the original drawn between a `q` and a `Q` of its own, the frame turned with /Rotate). Rebuilding the pages instead (FPDI's import) was tried and dropped: it lost what the Nachtrag draws outside its page content — its own „§22 53841*01“ in the margin of all 24 pages is a watermark annotation. Rendered and compared with pdf.js: both documents page for page, the margin mark kept. The seeder stores the copy under `gutachten/abnahme-{key}-beispiel.pdf` with the copy's fingerprint; its guard against doubling still compares the original's, so his document is still recognised. `rimify:demo --abnahme --beispiel-pdfs` gives a set already written its copies in place — same rows, same ids — checks each original by its fingerprint, reads the copy back, removes the old `abnahme-` file, writes one Protokoll line, and leaves a real document's file alone. | A copy that loses a line of its document is not that document; one that looks like the real one is a document we did not issue. — this commit |
| D-269 | 2026-10-17 | **Inertia's DevTools recorder is off unless asked for (`INERTIA_DEVTOOLS_ENABLED=true`) — the largest single cost of a local admin page (the operator's brief of 17.10.2026 §4).** Profiled through the HTTP kernel, a fresh application per request, signed in as the super-admin, with a probe between every middleware and every `RequestHandled` listener timed: on /admin/profil (541 ms in all) one listener took 362 ms — inertia-laravel's DevTools, which the package switches on for every `APP_ENV=local` when its setting is left unset, as ours was. It writes one entry per request to `storage/inertia-devtools` and prunes the folder after each response: 36 057 files, 1,24 GB since 21.09.2026, its 24-hour pruning having removed none. Every request of the browser suites ran on such a server. Production and the preview never ran it (their environment is not `local`). The block is the package's own, with only the switch defaulting off; `InertiaDevToolsOffTest` holds it off in a local environment. The local entries are still on the disk — 36 057 files, 1,21 GB in the main checkout's `storage/inertia-devtools`, 997 in the worktree's; this session may not delete under `storage/`, nothing reads them now, and removing them is a hand's job (corrected 17.10.2026 with D-270: this line said they were deleted). | A tool for looking at requests must not be the request's largest cost. — this commit |
| D-270 | 2026-10-17 | **The e2e servers' PHP workers get a compiled-code cache of their own, and each server its configuration, routes and events cached — the second the browser suites lost on every request was this machine's opcache, full (the operator's brief of 17.10.2026 §4).** Every built-in PHP server of one Windows user shares one opcache unless it names its own: 128 MB for both RIMIFY checkouts and other projects' servers, full (5 914 scripts, 0 MB free, the interned-string buffer full too), so nothing new was cached and every request compiled again what did not fit. Two-worker servers of the same checkout, 15 rounds each, alternating: `/up` — a route that does nothing — 639 ms shared against 37 ms with a cache of its own, the sign-in page 875 → 223 ms, the home page 1 092 → 342 ms, /felgen 995 → 280 ms. `serve.mjs` now names one cache per checkout (`opcache.cache_id`, 512 MB, 64 MB for interned strings — four pages alone filled 92 MB and 22 MB, where PHP's default buffer is 8 MB) and runs `config:cache`, `route:cache` and `event:cache` for each server into `storage/framework/cache/e2e-<port>/`, never `bootstrap/cache`, which the Pest suites would read — as `artisan optimize` does on the preview (`/up` 83 → 37 ms on top). The in-process profile saw neither: it keeps compiled classes between requests. `--no-cache` serves uncached; a route or config change needs a server restart. | A test server that compiles the framework on every request measures the machine, not the shop. — this commit |
| D-271 | 2026-10-17 | **Gutachten › Übersicht counts each document's rows first and joins the counts, and `fitments` carries `(approval_document_id, status)` in place of its one-column document index (the operator's brief of 17.10.2026 §4).** The list grouped every joined row by the document's eleven columns — a temporary table and a sort — and read each row's status from the table: 55–80 ms for 13 documents and 3 996 rows on the development database, the slowest single query of the five admin pages profiled, and growing with every Verwendungsbereich row entered. Counted first: 21 ms; from the index alone: 9 ms (the index added and dropped again on the development database to measure it). Same rows, same order, same counts. The old index was a prefix of the new one; the foreign key keeps two indexes led by the document (`uq_fitment_identity` and the new one). The reset baseline is rebuilt with the migration (89). | A list that slows with every row the client types is a list he stops opening. — this commit |
| D-272 | 2026-10-17 | **The rail's items get their tooltips once the page has hydrated; the server renders the items alone (the operator's brief of 17.10.2026 §4).** A CPU profile of the Node renderer over 100 admin renders gave the rail 64 % of the render time (9,5 of 14,8 s), much of it reka-ui's tooltip — some ten components for each of the thirty items — for tooltips that cannot open before hydration: nothing listens for the pointer yet. `AdminRailTooltip` draws the item alone until the rail is mounted, then inside its tooltip with the same markup; the toggle keeps its tooltip from the first frame. Two renderers, the same five page objects, 20 rounds alternating on a machine loaded by a full browser run: medians 503 → 263 ms (Profil), 536 → 351 (Gutachten), 754 → 495 (Felgen), 582 → 527 (Bestellungen), 716 → 575 (Übersicht). In a browser: no tooltip state in the server's HTML for any of 31 items, all 31 inside their tooltip after hydration, a collapsed rail's tooltip on hover, no hydration mismatch. | Work the server does for a control that cannot act before the browser takes over is work for nothing. — this commit |
| D-273 | 2026-10-17 | **EF-05 guards every call into the page against WebKit's stalls, the first load of the homepage included, and a browser that stalls on every attempt ends the test as `rig:` — not measured, not failed (the operator's brief of 17.10.2026 §5).** The run of 17.10.2026 counted EF-05 as not measured after 11½ minutes; its trace shows where: the first `page.goto('/')`, outside the stall guard, returned after 687 s, and not even the test's own five minutes could end it. The load now has a minute (a loaded machine's server may take seconds), and a stalled one starts again on a fresh page, twice at most, as a stalled step already did. A jump still fails the test. | A browser that drew nothing measured nothing; counting it as a fail would put a number on the gate that no assertion produced. — this commit |
| D-274 | 2026-10-17 | **A desktop admin page paints once the admin layout's last element is parsed, as a desktop storefront page does since D-177; the phone still paints at once.** The second full run on release 12 failed one assertion: the Übersicht at 768 px with the font held back, CLS 0,0392, nearly all of it `.adm-page__actions`. Five repeats on a quiet machine passed. The trace's frames showed why: on the loaded machine (CPU p90 88 %) Chrome painted the page with its header half-parsed — the Zeitraum's three buttons and the calendar icon beside the title, nothing after them, the body empty — and once the rest was parsed the whole switcher no longer fit there and dropped beneath the title. Not the font, and not D-272. D-177 had left the admin out; AdminLayout now ends with `#seitenende`, and the head's `rel="expect"` applies to every desktop page. The admin's sign-in and invitation pages have no layout and carry the element themselves, as the last child of their `<main>` — without it Chrome warns that the element it waited for never came, and the qa console gate failed on /admin/anmelden for that (the first full run on release 13). `AdminFirstPaintTest` holds the link, cls-budget the element's place. | A header painted before it is whole moves under the reader's eyes; where the parse outruns the paint anyway, holding it changes nothing. — this commit |
| D-275 | 2026-10-18 | **A dialog (`Ui/Dialog.vue`) builds reka-ui's dialog the first time it opens, then keeps it.** The admin's frame carries several dialogs on every page — the keyboard help, the review's feedback, a demo strip's confirmation — and hydration set each of them up for nothing: in a CPU profile of a cold Übersicht load the closed `AdminDemoBlocked` alone took 45 ms with its dialog's code on the stack (the operator's brief of 18.10.2026: profile the admin page). Opened once, a dialog stays built, so closing still plays out and hands the focus back. Measured honestly: the main thread's whole CPU for a cold load did not move beyond this machine's noise (Übersicht 1.589 → 1.602 ms, Gutachten 860 → 891 ms, four alternating loads each, another project's suite at full CPU beside it) — kept as work the page no longer does, not claimed as a speed-up. | Work done for a dialog nobody opened is work for nothing. — this commit |
| D-276 | 2026-10-18 | **The rail's item tooltips are set up when the browser is next idle after hydration (one second at most), not inside the task that hydrates the page.** D-272 drew them from mounting on; the profile of 18.10.2026 put reka-ui's tooltip machinery for the rail at about 150 ms of that task. `useWhenIdle()` holds them off until the browser has a moment: none can open before the pointer reaches the rail. It moves work out of the task that makes the page answerable; it does not remove it, and the whole load's CPU did not change measurably (D-275's numbers). | The first task after a page arrives should make it answerable, and nothing else. — this commit |
| D-277 | 2026-10-18 | **The top speed stays; each Gutachten says whether a car needs it. One question, read off the document, kept with its facts and asked before release: how does it give its tyres' load index and speed symbol — at every size, through the vehicle's papers, or through a table of tyre load at speed? (the operator's brief of 17.10.2026, „Do not remove the top speed. Make it conditional.“)** The client: „we actually dont need the vehicle top speed, so you can remove this field. And the axle loads are in the vehicle data … the needed speed index of the tire is explicit in the approval (teilegutachten, abe, …)“ — The Verwendungsbereich of his BBS Teilegutachten: „Der erforderliche Geschwindigkeits-/Lastindex ist den Fahrzeugpapieren zu entnehmen.“ ABE 55255, page 6: „Die mindestens erforderlichen Geschwindigkeitsbereiche (mit Ausnahme der M+S-Profile) und Tragfähigkeiten der zu verwendenden Reifen sind den Fahrzeugpapieren (Fahrzeugbrief und -schein, Zulassungsbescheinigung I) zu entnehmen. Abschläge der Tragfähigkeit aufgrund der Fahrzeughöchstgeschwindigkeit sind zu berücksichtigen.“, with its table, 210 to 300 km/h against V, W and Y, where „–“ is not permitted. The Nachtrag 366-0350-21-WIRD/N1, 10B on page 10: „Die mindestens erforderlichen Geschwindigkeitsbereiche der zu verwendenden Reifen sind, mit Ausnahme der Winterreifen Profile, den Fahrzeugpapieren zu entnehmen.“ Under all three the speed symbol a tyre needs follows from the car's top speed (field T); without it the table could only be read the permissive way. Where a document prints the index beside the size („mindestens 94Y“) the client is right. `approval_documents.tyre_index_source` — `document`, `papers` or `table`; null while unanswered, which reads the safe way, top speed needed; a CHECK holds the three — is entered with the facts. A release is refused while it is open, while „Tabelle“ has no table typed from this document (the demo seed's example is not one), and while „bei jeder Reifengröße“ leaves a size without its speed symbol. A document that prints a table and also points to the papers, as ABE 55255 does, is entered as „Tabelle“, the stricter; one that prints the symbol at only some sizes, as the Nachtrag does, as „Fahrzeugpapiere“. A base without rows of its own is not asked: its Nachtrag answers for its Verwendungsbereich. The acceptance set and the transcriptions carry ABE 55255 as „Tabelle“ and the Nachtrag as „Fahrzeugpapiere“; the test factory's default is „Fahrzeugpapiere“. Not changed yet, waiting on the operator's answer to the two questions this raises against R-03: that a car without a top speed is answered under „bei jeder Reifengröße“, and whether the tyre verdict where a document needs the top speed and the car has none is called „Noch nicht bestätigt“ (UNKNOWN) or stays CONDITIONAL. The vehicle form's conditional field follows those answers. | The document decides what a car must tell us — not the dataset and not a setting — and a question nobody has answered reads the safe way. — this commit |
| D-278 | 2026-10-18 | **`audit_logs` carries `(causer_type, causer_id, actor_email)` in one index in place of the morph's two-column `causer` (the operator's brief of 17.10.2026 §4).** The Protokoll's „Person“ filter lists everybody in the log once each — the distinct causer and address of every entry — and without an index holding all three the database read the whole log into a temporary table on every visit. The log only grows (R-12). Measured on copies of the development log (13 176 entries, 17 people): MariaDB 11.8.9, the production engine, 49,2 ms → 0,69 ms; MySQL 8.4.9 23,2 → 1,05 ms (medians of 25) — the plan goes from a full scan to `Using index for group-by`, one step per person, whatever the log's length. The old index is a prefix of the new one; every query by causer keeps its index. Indexes only: no row of the append-only log is written. | A page that reads the whole log to list seventeen names gets slower every day the shop is used. — this commit |
| D-279 | 2026-10-18 | **An admin page's own script chunks are preloaded in the head — fetched beside the entry at the scripts' low priority, compiled, never run there (`App\Support\PageModules`); the shop's pages not yet (the operator's brief of 17.10.2026 §4).** The bundle asked for a page's chunk only once the entry had run and resolved the page: one more round trip before the page answers, on every first load. Measured against the same HTML with the links stripped — same server and build, alternating, cold cache, 6–8 loads each, the network throttled in Chromium: at 100 ms and 20 Mbit/s the Übersicht hydrated 0,15–0,6 s sooner and Gutachten 0,15–0,38 s, the Protokoll no differently; at 5 Mbit/s 0,25 and 0,23 s; on a throttled phone (150 ms, 1,6 Mbit/s, 4× CPU) the Übersicht 4,2 s sooner; the first paint never came later beyond the noise. The shop's pages gained as well (the homepage 0,6–1,0 s on the desk), but their LCP is a picture, and on this machine's HTTP/1.1 server, six connections deep, the chunks queued ahead of it: /felgen's LCP 0,2–0,6 s later, the phone homepage's 0,9 s — about twice what two identical variants differed by. They stay as they were until it is measured over the preview's HTTP/2. Not a module script: that ran the page's code on its own (PageStyles). | A page that waits a round trip for its own code waits for nothing; a picture that waits behind code nobody needs yet makes a slower page. — this commit |
| D-280 | 2026-10-18 | **The Übersicht computes each figure when it is asked for, so the request that brings its charts computes the charts alone (the operator's brief of 17.10.2026 §4).** The charts come a moment after the page, in a request of their own (Inertia's deferred props) — and that request ran the whole controller again: every sales tile, the work lists, Top-Felgen and the matrix's row count were computed eagerly before the page was rendered, then dropped, because only `charts` was asked for. Profiled in-process, the charts request spent 211 ms in middleware and controller, nearly all of it on figures it threw away; with each figure a closure, 60 ms. Over HTTP on this machine, medians of 12: the charts request 222 → 156 ms; the page itself unchanged (172 → 167 ms) — it still computes everything. The sales figures two props share are computed once. A test records each request's queries: Top-Felgen's and the work lists' run with the page and never with the charts — it fails against the old controller. | A second request that redoes the first one's work to throw it away only makes the page settle later. — this commit |
| D-281 | 2026-10-18 | **The admin shell (`admin`: the rail and its counts, the account, the preferences) is resolved with the response, not in `share()` (the operator's brief of 17.10.2026 §4).** A partial reload — the Übersicht's charts, a Gutachten's preview — asks for its own props, and Inertia leaves the shell out of its answer; it was computed all the same, 26,8 ms and 12 queries of an admin request (profiled per part of `share()`). Now a closure: a full page resolves it exactly as before, a partial reload never computes it. History encryption stays on every admin response. With D-280 the charts request ran 57 queries before and 26 now (in-process, the super-admin); the page's own request is unchanged. A test records the queries of each request: the rail's counts run with the page and never with the reload — it fails against the old middleware. | What a response will not carry should not be computed for it. — this commit |
| D-282 | 2026-10-18 | **D-279 extends to every page on the desk; on the phone it stays with the admin's pages — the shop's phone pages keep no page-chunk preload.** Measured on the preview, read-only and without a deploy: the shop's pages as served against the same HTML with the links D-279 would write (computed from the preview's public build manifest as `PageModules` does, injected by the browser's own route), alternating, cold cache, the host's real HTTP/2 from here. On the desk (8 loads each): the homepage hydrated 3,07 → 2,63 s and /felgen 3,10 → 2,55 s; the first paint unchanged (1,59 → 1,60 s; 1,64 → 1,60 s), the LCP no later (2,51 → 2,12 s; 2,15 → 2,20 s). The LCP cost D-279 measured on this machine was its HTTP/1.1 server queueing on six connections. On a throttled phone (1,6 Mbit/s, 4× CPU, 7 loads each) the homepage gained nothing (6,77 → 6,73 s) and its LCP moved 2,35 → 2,46 s; /felgen hydrated 0,64 s sooner, its LCP 3,27 → 3,44 s with a wider spread — the phone's LCP is its first measure, and it is not traded for that. | Measured where it runs: what the local server showed was the local server. — this commit |
| D-283 | 2026-10-18 | **An admin page no longer carries the shop's frame — header mode, menus, mega menu, basket, garage, consent and the rest of `Storefront\Chrome` (the operator's brief of 17.10.2026 §4).** Every admin request computed it and sent it along: 3,4 KB of the Übersicht's 18,1 KB of props and of Gutachten's 12,8 KB (`menus` 2,2 KB, `mega` 0,9 KB), and 8,3 ms and three queries in `share()`. No admin page, layout or composable reads any of it; an admin page with a prop of the same name (Navigation's `menus`, a vehicle's `vehicle`) sends its own. Decided by the same check as the admin shell (`isAdmin`), so the two never disagree. Two tests, one request each — Inertia keeps shared props in one object per application, and a second request in a test would find the first one's: the admin page carries none of the frame, a shop page all of it; the first fails against the old middleware. | What a page never draws is neither computed for it nor sent. — this commit |
| D-284 | 2026-10-18 | **Under a Gutachten that prints the index beside every tyre size, the tyres are answered without the car's top speed; under one that needs it, a car whose axle loads are known is told the one field it lacks, and why (the operator's brief of 17.10.2026, „Do not remove the top speed. Make it conditional.“, and the operator's answer of 18.10.2026 to the first of the two questions it put to R-03: yes).** Where every document covering the combination answers „bei jeder Reifengröße“ (D-277) and every offered size carries both its printed figures — load index and a symbol the table knows, „91W“ —, the symbol is the document's and stands alone, no table is read, and the load index is the stricter of the document's and the axle load's at full load, the floor (R-06); the lines say so: „Die Achslast verlangt Tragfähigkeitsindex 88 (560 kg). | D-291 | 2026-10-18 | **The papers form asks for what is missing, never for all three (the operator's brief of 18.10.2026, §3: „The shape of the row has no business deciding the shape of the question.“).** Each figure is one of three things: asked — the answer waits for it (`tyre.owed`, now per axle: where our data knows 8.1 and not 8.2, only 8.2) —, a required field; known and not asked, shown read-only with where it came from, „aus unseren Fahrzeugdaten“ or „von dir eingetragen“, never asked again — as plain text on the field's line, not a locked field (the look review); neither, a voluntary field where nothing is asked and not shown where something is. Our data's figures are not copied into it: „Deine Angaben“ — on the panel and on the printed RIMIFY-Check — never names a figure the customer did not give, and a corrected data figure is never shadowed by a stale copy. The default, decided now as the operator asked: the figures in a check are stored only to produce it, so they expire with it — `CheckResult` is pruned after `KEEP_MONTHS` = 12, the cookie's year, by the nightly `model:prune` beside the contact messages (its UPDATE trigger stays; a DELETE was never refused). The check page says „Link gültig bis …“, and a link that opens nothing says „Dieses Ergebnis können wir nicht anzeigen.“ and why that may be — the old sentence, „Ergebnis-Links verfallen, wenn sich die zugrunde liegende Freigabe geändert hat“, was not what the code did, and „nicht mehr verfügbar“ claimed a history a mistyped link never had. The retention question for the Kanzlei is `docs/client-questions.md` #38, beside #36 and #37. | „A stored figure with no expiry is a decision nobody made.“ — this commit |
| D-293 | 2026-10-18 | **D-289 to D-292 after their interaction review — 12 findings, every one fixed but one rule, which goes to the operator.** (1) A figure our data holds is read-only as asked, but never a way closed to a stricter answer: „Steht in deinen Papieren ein anderer Wert?“ opens it as a field („In unseren Fahrzeugdaten: 1.210 kg. Wir rechnen mit dem höheren der beiden Werte.“), and a photo that reads another figure than ours opens it with the reading in it — an Auflastung would otherwise have been answered for the lighter axle (CLAUDE.md §2 over the instruction's default). (2) The figure shown is the one that governs, the larger of ours and the customer's, with its source. (3) Where the customer's own figure makes the answer negative — a mistyped 1.610 kg over a wheel tested for 700 kg —, the papers form stays, and the refusal names the figure's source: „Maßgeblich ist die zulässige Achslast der Vorderachse: 1.610 kg nach deiner Angabe aus Feld 8.1, also 805 kg je Rad …“ — or „aus unseren Fahrzeugdaten“. (4) A rim line that stopped selling says why in the add's words (`Basket::verdictRefusal`), never „Für diese Kombination liegt uns kein Gutachten vor“ over an approved wheel (R-17). (5) An empty save where only 15.1/15.2 show is said under 15.1: „Trag die Reifen aus den Feldern 15.1 und 15.2 ein oder schließ das Formular mit „Abbrechen“.“ — and, where the customer gave figures before, how to delete them. (6) A photo's reading is forgotten on „Abbrechen“; a reading that lands after the page left lands nowhere, and a worker that loads after its form went is ended; the form sends the keyboard only within itself. (7) The RIMIFY-Check's Komplettrad mode reads „Werte eintragen“ where the tyres wait for the papers, as the product page does. (8) The wheel guard leaves Ctrl+wheel and sideways swipes alone. (9) A printed check lives through the whole last day its page names, in Berlin, goes the night after, and is treated as gone once past it even before the prune runs; one whose stored answer cannot be read says so, not the expiry. (10) The papers form follows a size change (closed where the rim sells alone, untouched; open where the answer waits). (11) The rim alone's sentence is part of what the checkout records as shown. **Left to the operator:** where the tyres are unconfirmed for want of our own record (a table not entered, finding 103) the rim does not sell alone; where only the papers are missing it does — so on a fast car whose document has no table on record, entering the papers turns a rim that sold into one that does not. | Each finding read and measured against the code; the rule is the operator's (§9: what is sold where legality is concerned). — this commit |
| D-294 | 2026-10-18 | **No answer of the shop is kept anywhere on its way, and the shop's own caches are kept under the catalogue's version (his admin item 16, Group B — S1: a kept page can show a verdict after its Gutachten was corrected).** Measured on the preview before: `Cache-Control: no-cache, private` — the framework's default; a browser keeps the page, and Back shows it without asking —, no ETag, nothing for LiteSpeed; the CDN passed pages through (`x-hcdn-cache-status: DYNAMIC`). Five caches of the shop's own held answers for 1 to 15 minutes, forgotten only on a release: the vehicle tree, a car's count, the menu, the search, the homepage's figures. Now every answer the application computes says `no-store, private` and `X-LiteSpeed-Cache-Control: no-cache` (`CacheHeaders`, in the global stack), `Vary` gains `Cookie` (the car and its papers; the filters are in the address), and the ETag names the catalogue's version and the answer's content, `W/"k<version>-<xxh3>"`; an answer that chose its own rule keeps it (the postcode lookup's day, an invoice's `no-store`, files). `public/.htaccess` tells LiteSpeed the same for every address that reaches the application. The version (`CatalogueVersion`) is renewed after every committed write to the 24 models the answers rest on, and by the bulk writes past them — a release, a take-back, an import and its rollback, a conflict decided, a review's rating, the demo set, the test reset —; never for a write rolled back. The five caches keep their entries under it. Two copies the headers never reach: a page brought back by Back or Forward — Inertia's history or the browser's back/forward cache — asks for the car and the version alone and is asked again whole where either moved on (`restoredPages.ts`, which takes over the check of `vehicleMemory.ts`); a page fetched ahead on hover is used for 5 seconds, not Inertia's 30. A stale page could never put a stale verdict on an order: the basket and the checkout compute theirs anew. | The operator's Group B as written, plus the two client-side copies; `CacheHeadersTest` fetches, changes, fetches. — this commit |
| D-295 | 2026-10-18 | **His admin items 9 and 15, finished where §1 found them half done.** **9:** beside each tyre size of a Verwendungsbereich row, the document's minimum stands in two fields of its own — the load index typed as a whole number, the speed symbol chosen from the server's table, L to (Y) — instead of one string per size; the server already took and checked them apart (`FitmentRowsRequest`, D-285 at release). A size pasted as the PDF prints it, „245/35 R19 93Y“ (XL, RF read past), settles into the three fields on leaving the size field, and a front-and-rear pair pasted into one, „225/40 R18 92Y / 255/35 R18 94Y“, becomes two lines, front and rear; every save settles first, so what is sent is what the fields show, and a load index that does not read stops the save at its own field — nothing typed is dropped. **15:** refused as asked, granted as meant (the operator's A.4): the table of tyre load at speed stands whole only where the record says the document prints one (D-277) — the release waits for it there — or where a table is entered all the same; on every other document it folds to one line, „Keine Tabelle hinterlegt“, opened: finding 104's sentence and why none is asked for, from the recorded answer only (R-17). Stored as `manuell_geprueft`, never `entwicklung`; one Protokoll entry; in the selector at once (D-294). After the interaction review: HSN and TSN read past every space and never cut to length by the field (` 0005` was saved as 0000), a month typed with a comma (a phone's decimal keypad), years from 1900 to two years ahead, a removed twin said as removed, and the error's focus set after the dialog has closed. | His items 1 and 3; the field list is the engine's, as the brief sets it. — this commit |
| D-297 | 2026-10-18 | **Fahrzeuge › Import: a vehicle dataset into the shop, keyed on HSN, TSN and variant, a Probelauf with counts before anything is written (his admin item 2, Group C).** The file and its source (KBA-Typdaten, TecDoc or DAT — every car it creates is stamped `import_*`), its columns mapped (by name, or as the last file of that source had them, or by hand), each row planned against the cars as they are: neu; geändert or unverändert where a car of an earlier import of the same source carries the key (a figure the file leaves empty never empties one); ergänzt where a car typed from a Gutachten fits by EG number and Fahrzeugtyp — the merge (VehicleMerge) fills its empty fields, HSN and TSN among them, rather than a second car without the Gutachten's rows; bleibt where a person checked the car, a Gutachten named it, it was removed (R-04) or it came from another source — a source is never changed; abgelehnt where key numbers or the name are missing, a row or a dataset number stands twice, or two Gutachten cars fit. A car that carries key numbers — typed from its papers, or completed before — takes in only a row under the same ones: another HSN or TSN is another car and becomes one, so no car's axle loads are ever filled from another variant's (a class fix in VehicleMerge); a match with nothing left to fill counts as unverändert. A figure that does not read is stored as nothing and said, never guessed (R-03); body and drive only from the lists the Gutachten conditions use. Dates as datasets and German spreadsheets write them; a build end given as a month ends on its last day (R-02) — the merge reads it the same way now. An imported car without a dataset number gets the next free one (`chk_veh_export_id_from_source`). Applying plans again first and writes nothing if the cars changed since. One transaction, one Protokoll entry. Boundary: 5.000 rows and 5 MB a file, as the catalogue's import. His demo file is not with us: the mapping reads any CSV or Excel file instead of guessing his columns. After the reviews: the counts named as the catalogue's run names its own („Neue Fahrzeuge“ … „Nicht überschrieben“), a nought quiet and a refusal red — the catalogue's run page takes the same emphasis —, a refused row's next step said, a row without both key numbers shows „–“; both imports' refusal names the button that redoes the Probelauf („Wähl „Neu prüfen““), and a run with nothing to bring in says „Nichts zu importieren“ in the list, not „Zuordnung unvollständig“. After the interaction review (one fail-open among them): a build start that is missing or does not read, or an end given and unread, refuses the row — stored as nothing it would be an open window, a car built at any time (R-02) —, so „Gebaut ab“ is a required column; what the table would refuse (an end before the start, text over its length) is refused in the Probelauf with its reason, never a run that fails at „Importieren“; the run is imported under its own lock and once — a second click or tab is told the file is imported, not that a right is missing —, and the page sends the fingerprint of the Probelauf it shows, so a mapping changed in another tab asks for a reload; plans are compared key-sorted, as the catalogue's (a MySQL JSON column re-sorts keys); a dataset's car numbers are unique within their source (`uq_vehicle_source_number`) and a number the shop hands out is never one the file brings; a UTF-16 file (Excel's „Unicode-Text“) is read as such, by both imports; the run list reads summaries, not files. | His item 2 and the operator's import rules, word for word; the merge's rule kept so no Gutachten car is doubled. — this commit |

| D-306 | 2026-10-19 | **A Blog article is built from blocks — Abschnitt, Bild, Tabelle, Zitat — in any order; there is no free HTML (his admin item 11, Group F).** He asked for photo upload and HTML for sections, tables for instance. A picture is uploaded in the editor at once — the panel's own file picker, dropped or chosen — into our storage (`media_site`, `blog/{slug}`), checked by ImageUpload (type from its bytes, size, sides, no EXIF), its width and height kept so the page reserves its room; only an address of our storage passes the publish check, never one on another site. Its description (alt text) is required, its caption optional. A table has a caption (required, read first by a screen reader), 2 to 6 columns with a heading each, 1 to 30 rows of as many cells, each cell at most 200 characters, typed cell by cell; the buttons stop where the check would refuse. A quote has its words and, optionally, who said them; the page sets it as a `<blockquote>` with a rule at its edge, never a box. The publish check names each fault at its place („Tabelle 1 · Inhalt: Jede Spalte braucht einen Spaltenkopf.“), the dialog before publishing names each new block, and the Protokoll records every upload. Only an article takes pictures (another page answers 404), and only who may edit it may upload (PagePolicy). | His item 11, solved differently: the blocks he named, without the risk free HTML carries. — this commit |

| D-308 | 2026-10-19 | **After the interaction review of 211: a block keeps its identity, a double-click is one click, and changes are named per kind of block.** (1) The editor keys every block by the block itself, not its place: a picture still uploading moves with its block, and a block removed takes its upload with it (aborted, never answered into the block that took its place — SEI-23). (6) An upload refused for another reason than the file says which: the sign-in expired, the right is gone, the file is too large, too many at once (SEI-27). (7) The shop prints no table row left empty, leaves out a picture whose file does not load, and gives a shared article's picture its full address. | D-310 | 2026-10-19 | **The load-index table runs to 120, index by index from its published source (the operator's brief of 19.10.2026 §1).** **Extending the table grants no permission: a Gutachten still has to name the size for the car.** It only stops us refusing a heavy car on a limit of our own — beyond 1.030 kg a tyre the table had no index, the derivation answered null and the car got „Noch nicht bestätigt“ for every tyre. The source: Annex IV „Load-capacity indices“ of UN/ECE Regulation No 30, the passenger-car tyres' regulation (Official Journal L 201, 30.7.2008, p. 90). The 40 entries we had (70–109) are its figures, 40 of 40 — the spec's Appendix B, which they came from, is not in the repository, so the annex is the published source they are held to; it prints 110 to 120, eleven indices (1.060 to 1.400 kg), and nothing above. Annex IV of Regulation No 54 (OJ L 183, 11.7.2008, pp. 57–60), a second publication, prints the same eleven values (11 of 11). Both annexes are kept as their text extracted by pdf.js (`tests/Fixtures/reference/`, with the URL and the PDF's SHA-256), and `LoadIndexSourceTest` holds every constant to both — never a figure typed a second time. **No index above 120 is offered:** Regulation No 30 prints none, so the tyre form refuses it with the reason („Für einen Lastindex über 120 hat der Shop keine Tragfähigkeit hinterlegt …“), a document row naming one is refused („… kennt der Shop nicht. Er rechnet mit den Indizes 70 bis 120.“ — no longer „gibt es nicht“, which said more than we know), and a car needing more than 1.400 kg a tyre stays „Noch nicht bestätigt“. **The cross-check, re-run:** ABE 55255's fourteen load-index limits are still exactly twice their capacity, 14 of 14; no document on record names an index from 101 to 120 by its code, so none of the new indices could be checked against a printed limit (0 of 11) — the release check does it for any document that names one. **So the eleven are sourced, not confirmed: 0 of 11 is confirmed by our own cross-check** (the operator's brief of 19.10.2026, standby). The first document entered that prints any of T10 to T20 triggers a re-check of all eleven against what it prints; if any fails, all eleven come out of the table — this migration's `down()`, which stops where a row still names one of them, so those are looked at first — until the difference is understood. How it shows: the release check holds every printed load-index limit to twice its capacity and blocks the document's release until a person confirms a differing figure with a written reason (`LoadLimitCheck`); for T10 to T20 that confirmation is not the end of it — the eleven come out first. **One reading fixed on the way, in the strict direction:** a code is the index's last two digits — T00 is 100 (ABE 55255) — but T01 to T69 were read as 1 to 69: a limit „T12 bis 2.240 kg“ would have bound no tyre and passed the release check unread. They are 101 to 169 now (T12 = 112, held to 2 × 1.120 kg at release; T21 = 121 has no capacity, and no tyre meets it). The rows reach an existing database by a data migration that writes this one table from the same constant, not by raising ReleaseSeed's version, which would have run all seven of its seeders on the client's preview for eleven rows; the reset baseline is written again (96 migrations). **The migration extends only a table that holds rows:** its first version also filled a new database's empty one, CatalogueSeeder — which seeds the reference data while the load-index table is empty — took it for seeded, the speed symbols stayed empty, and 473 Feature tests stopped on ReferenceDataMissing; the gate caught it before any commit. A new database gets both tables from ReferenceDataSeeder as before, and the seeder's guard now looks at both (`LoadIndexMigrationTest`). | The operator's answer; the conservative reading everywhere the table ends. — this commit |

| D-314 | 2026-10-19 | **#208 on his preview is „not measured“, never „failed“ (the operator's brief of 19.10.2026 §4.4–4.5).** The admin list's pass on release 20 failed #208 in both engines: his preview's example ABE 55255 was seeded on 14.10, before the section „Bereifungskombinationen“ existed (D-256, 16.10), so none is entered there, and entering them takes a draft — a write the pass never makes on his preview. The page said so truthfully („Zu diesem Gutachten sind keine Bereifungskombinationen hinterlegt.“). A failure would have read as a defect in the shop; a pass would have claimed what nobody saw. On the preview the spec now checks the section, its rule and that sentence, and records the list itself as not measured, with the reason; on our shop it still requires the entered list. His sheet's row says what he can do to see it. | Three states, each meaning what it says (the brief of 17.10.2026 §5). — this commit |
| D-315 | 2026-10-19 | **His demo data's `fahrzeug_max_kmh` is read as field T by the import (the operator's brief of 19.10.2026, from the client's message of the same day — D-287).** It stands among the names the import finds T under without being told, right after the contract's own `hoechstgeschwindigkeit_kmh`, and `docs/launch/FAHRZEUGDATEN.md` — the column mapping, written from the code field by field — names it, so his twenty-row sample has something to compare against. Kilometres per hour as a whole number from 60 to 420, as for every source and as the customer's papers form reads it (`PapersFigures`). **Zero, blank or implausible is never imported as a number**, and, as CLAUDE.md R-03 requires, the row is not refused for it either: the car is stored without a top speed, the Probelauf names the value it could not read, and the car waits under „Zur Prüfung“, its tyres „Noch nicht bestätigt“ wherever the Gutachten needs T. Refusing the whole row, as the brief's wording had it, would also drop a car whose wheels a Gutachten approves. **The operator confirmed on 20.10.2026 that R-03 stands and his wording was wrong:** it contradicted R-03 and his own rule from the top-speed brief that a missing input never produces a refusal. The row imports, no bogus figure is stored, the car sits under „Zur Prüfung“ — nothing to amend. BLOCKED.md #16 no longer calls the import unbuilt. | The column he named, read; R-03 as it stands. — this commit |
| D-316 | 2026-10-19 | **A document about the shop for readers outside it — a law firm, an authority, the client — is written from the code, and every fact in it is checked there before it is written (the operator's brief of 19.10.2026, standby).** KANZLEI.md was written that way and found four defects in the shop itself (D-313): the cookie table lacked `rmf_view` and Stripe's own cookies while the settings say every cookie stands there, Stripe's cookies come when the payment step opens rather than at payment, `rmf_view` is set only by a link, and `rmf_papiere` has a second delete button the table did not name. A legal document generated from the implementation audits the implementation; one written from memory or from an older document repeats its errors. Where a document can be generated, it is (VERARBEITUNG.md, from the installation's configuration); where it cannot, it names the files it rests on, as KANZLEI.md's last line does. | The operator's rule; the cheapest audit there is. — this commit |
| D-317 | 2026-10-20 | **The Probelauf of a vehicle import counts what the file lacks, not only what it imports — as counts and as shares of the file (the operator's brief of 20.10.2026).** Over every row of the file, whatever becomes of the row: how many have no usable top speed (T — `fahrzeug_max_kmh` in his data), how many lack 8.1 or 8.2, how many lack HSN or TSN, how many lack the EG number (K). A value that is missing and one that does not read count alike — for the shop they are the same. Shown under the Probelauf's counts as „7 von 20 · 35 %“, the share formatted on the server through the one helper (`GermanFormat::percent`, R-10); a run checked before the gaps were counted shows none until it is checked again. This turns his twenty-row sample into a purchasing decision: if a third of the dataset comes without axle loads, he has that number before he pays, not after. Written into `docs/launch/FAHRZEUGDATEN.md`'s twenty-row check. | The operator's brief: the number he decides on, before the money. — this commit |
| D-318 | 2026-10-20 | **Two rules for every share of a file the shop shows, not only this one (the operator's answer of 20.10.2026).** **First: the denominator is every row of the file, refused rows included** — excluded, a half-refused file would show flattering shares. **Second: „Keine Spalte zugeordnet“ is not „fehlt im Datensatz“.** The shop reports what it mapped and what it read, never what his file does or does not contain (R-17); the line holds if anyone asks to simplify the table. My report of D-317 said the page told the two apart; it did not — a field without a column showed as „Fehlt in 20 von 20 · 100 %“ under „Was in der Datei fehlt“, explained only in the note below. Now the row itself says „Keine Spalte zugeordnet“ (or „Keine Spalte für Feld 8.2 zugeordnet“ where one of a pair has none), with no count and no share. **The sample and the full dataset, on record:** the twenty rows are run as a Probelauf on our own shop — never a release for it; release 20 stays until his lists are closed and the sample is confirmed, then everything since goes out in one release —, their four shares written into `docs/launch/FAHRZEUGDATEN.md` with the date and the file's name; the full dataset is run the same way later and compared with them, and the comparison is recorded either way, a match included. A sample showing 0 % missing axle loads beside a full file at 30 % was not representative of what he bought: a finding he gets in writing, not one we find while debugging a verdict. **The same principle from the other side: a number we cannot justify is worse than a number we admit we do not have** (the operator's brief of 20.10.2026, second answer). A column can be mapped to the wrong column: 8.1 on the total mass reads fine in every row, the Probelauf shows 0 % missing, and the shop would compute axle-load verdicts from a number that is no axle load — a silent, confidently wrong path, the very one the twenty-row sample exists to catch. So the Probelauf has a **third state beside missing and mapped: read, but implausible** — „Gelesen, aber unplausibel“, four checks, each with up to three rows as read: an axle load outside 400 to 2.500 kg (the operator's band; it replaces the import's earlier 300 to 4.000); 8.1 equal to 8.2 in every row, judged only from three rows with both on (one or two such cars exist), with the one column named when both are mapped to it; 8.1 plus 8.2 against the car's total mass (F.2, a new column read for this check alone and never stored), believed from 95 to 140 % — the axle loads must carry the mass, so a car's sum lies about 100 to 125 % of it, while one axle load mapped to the mass itself gives about 150 to 165 %; and the top speed against the 60 to 420 km/h band the customer's papers form already holds it to (`PapersFigures`), now referenced instead of written a second time. Each finding is phrased as what was read and what was expected, never as a fact about his file: „Gelesen: 2.600 kg. Erwartet für eine Achslast: 400 bis 2.500 kg.“ A check that could run on no row says so instead of a nought (TEST-SMELLS 2), and one without its column says „Keine Spalte zugeordnet“. **It warns and never stops:** no row is refused, the import is not blocked, the confirmation says what was found — the fix is a mapping change, not a data change. A single value outside its band is not stored (the car waits under „Zur Prüfung“, R-03); the values behind a cross-row finding are believable one by one and stored as read, and the person importing decides. | The operator's rules; R-17 holds for files as for documents. — this commit |
| D-319 | 2026-10-23 | **The legal texts and the Impressum are placeholders until the client replaces them, built so a placeholder can never be taken for the real text or reach the public by accident (the client's decision, the operator's brief of 23.10.2026).** *The Impressum renders from the admin fields* of Einstellungen › Verkäufer & Versand — company name, Rechtsform (new), address, Geschäftsführung, Registergericht, Registernummer, USt-IdNr., Telefon, E-Mail, each entered once; an empty field shows its marker („[FIRMENNAME]“, „[RECHTSFORM]“ …) and nothing is invented. A field that honestly does not apply — Geschäftsführung, Register, USt-IdNr., Telefon — is declared „Entfällt“ and the row is left out: a declaration, never a value; a value and „Entfällt“ at once is refused beside the field, since which is meant is not ours to guess. *One notice per text:* AGB, Widerrufsbelehrung, Muster-Widerrufsformular, Datenschutzerklärung — and, failing closed, the Impressum's own text and the Versand page — each have a placeholder flag (`pages.is_placeholder`, default on, so a legal page made any way starts as one; the form, which has no page, a setting that is on while absent). While it is on, the page, the order mail and its PDFs carry the one notice „PLATZHALTER: Dieser Text wird vor der Veröffentlichung durch den Rechtstext der Kanzlei ersetzt.“ and not one word of the text; the cookie table on the Datenschutz page stays (facts, not a clause), and the team's signed preview shows the words. Footer links and the checkout's references stay real. *The switch* sits on the legal page in Inhalte › Seiten, for whoever may publish it, behind a question, one Protokoll entry each; it never goes off over the seeder's marker, publishing never turns it off, and publishing the marker again turns it back on. *Three locks* while any flag is on or any Impressum field is empty, all reading one answer (LegalReadiness): the shop sends `X-Robots-Tag: noindex, nofollow` and robots.txt says `Disallow: /`, in production too; `rimify:go-live-check` fails those items and exits non-zero for them although they are the client's (GO-LIVE.md already asks for exit 0), and the preflight's row reads the same answer; Einstellungen › Zahlung refuses Livemodus in a German sentence naming each missing text and field — Testmodus and test keys keep working. Files under /storage carry no header in production (the web server serves them); robots.txt covers them. Every list of what is missing names the fields as the admin form does („Geschäftsführung“, „USt-IdNr.“), not as the Impressum prints them. KANZLEI.md is unchanged; two questions from the copy review stay open for the Kanzlei, through the operator: whether the contact form counts as the second fast way of contact (§ 5 DDG) where Telefon is „Entfällt“, and whether „Entfällt“ for the Geschäftsführung fits the client's Rechtsform (a GbR or KG is still represented by someone). | The client's decision; fails closed (CLAUDE.md §2); R-11, R-16. — this commit |

| D-335 | 2026-10-24 | **Where the Gutachten lists its tyre combinations, the Komplettrad is the combination — two wheels per axle in the sizes it names, never four of one size (the operator's decision of 24.10.2026 on the admin audit's F-10; the source is the client's item 8, ADMINLISTE 208 / ADMIN-GEFUNDEN 515, whose combinations 2 and 3 have different sizes front and rear).** Rule 2b of the Komplettrad eligibility refuses four of a kind wherever the documents list their combinations (`AsCombination`); rules 4b and 7b of D-256 and their two refusals go with it. Every combination the car's row carries — each size on the axle the combination names it for, the same size on both axles included — is offered as a set, 2 + 2 and fixed: the basket holds it as one entry of two axle lines at two each whatever was posted, adding it again keeps the one set, and no stepper is shown; a size the row gives one axle is never four and never on the other axle. Each axle is answered on its own (`TyreEligibility::combinationVerdict()`, `AxleVerdict`): its size, the tyre against that axle's own load — 8.1 front, 8.2 rear — and the top speed (R-03), its Auflagen, never stronger than the wheel's verdict; the set is the weaker axle, so a rear that fails above 210 km/h is NOT_PERMITTED whatever the front. Where the offer's own gate refuses — a tyre rule the engine does not read, the car's figures not settled (R-03), a frozen verdict — no axle is answered on its tyre: UNKNOWN with the gate's reason, never CONDITIONAL. The papers are the set's: the car carries both sizes, so each line of a set says the Abnahme or Eintragung either axle's size needs (`ConditionGroups::entryForSet()`, `PackageTyreSizes::partner()`) — in the basket, on the order page, in the mail and in the admin, where the front line had said „Keine Eintragung erforderlich.“ beside a rear that needs one (the interaction review). A set is removed, loses its tyres („Reifen beider Achsen entfernen“) and takes its Wuchtgewichte colour as one, so each is offered once, under its second line; added again, it says „Dieser Satz ist schon im Warenkorb“. A combination the shop has no tyre for on one axle names that axle and has no set; nothing fills the gap, and the rim alone stays buyable. The product page and the RIMIFY-Check offer the combinations in the Komplettrad panel in place of the tyres; the Mischbereifung section stays for a printed table (V19). Each axle's Auflagen stand under „Vorderachse“ and „Hinterachse“ (as each size's under its own, D-199) — on the product page, in the basket, on the order page, in the confirmation mail and in the admin's order —, where a set's two lines had shown the rear tyre's size for both (`PackageTyreSizes` read one tyre per package). The order line freezes the combination's row (`document_tyre_combinations.id`), its number and page, and both axles — size, tyre, the answer and what it was measured against, the Auflagen — on each of the set's two lines (`verdict.combination`), and the order writer refuses a set whose answer does not sell. | D-336 | 2026-10-24 | **What makers and profile types a set's tyres may come in is the document's own recorded rule, never ours (the operator's decision of 24.10.2026, R-17).** `TyreLineRule` reads the sentence after a pairing table strictly, as the table itself is read: ABE 55255's V19, „Es sind nur Reifen eines Herstellers und achsweise eines Profiltyps zulässig“ — one maker on every tyre, one profile type on each axle; „Es sind vorn und hinten nur Reifen eines Herstellers und Typs zulässig“ — one maker and one type on every tyre; a sentence that speaks of makers or profiles in words not read leaves the Auflage unread (rule 2, `TyreChoiceRestricted`), and a scope word left beside a clause read („achsweise“) does too — never half a rule. The engine had one hard-coded rule for every document — the same maker, tyre line and season front and rear — and refused what V19 allows: two tyre lines of one maker, two seasons; and beside a list that prints no rule (D-256) any two makers (`CombinationNotOneMaker`, removed). Read as V19 words it, „achsweise“ binds the profile type alone: one maker on every tyre stays asked, so two makers stay refused under V19. The refusal says the rule plainly and quotes the recorded clause, a cut marked „[…]“ („Die Reifen vorne und hinten müssen vom selben Hersteller sein. Im Gutachten steht dazu: „…““); `MixedNotOneProfile` for a document asking one type on every tyre. Ours, said as ours and never a refusal: the offer pairs one season front and rear, and puts one maker's one line first. A set of two lines names each axle's tyre (Komplettrad panel, RIMIFY-Check, Mischbereifung cards, EU labels). The copy review's wording; „Profil“ for the type, the word the admin's field and the card use. | The operator asked whether the one-maker rule over-refuses: the check found it right for V19 and three over-refusals beside it, all from one rule no document recorded. — this commit |
| D-337 | 2026-10-24 | **No test reads the calendar for data written on another day (the operator's decision of 24.10.2026, after UEB-02).** The browser suite's global setup writes the admin's demo set again on today's date wherever it ends on another (`demo:reanchor`, 675 s, so the first run of a day pays it) and hands its last day to the specs (`demo:anchor`, `E2E_DEMO_ANCHOR`). A day-bound spec asks the server to answer on that day: `X-E2E-Today`, `App\Http\Middleware\E2eToday` — only with `RIMIFY_E2E_CLOCK=on`, which the suite sets for the servers it starts, only where APP_ENV is local or testing, and inside the session and the CSRF token, so neither cookie is ever dated on the frozen day. UEB-01, UEB-02 and M1-06 (`openUebersicht`), UEB-05, UEB-06 and UEB-07 (`openNachfrage`) and the filled Übersicht shot run on it; UEB-03, UEB-04 and UEB-08 bring their own data and read no day. `e2e/bugs/e2e-today.spec.ts` proves the wiring on a day the calendar is not. `docs/dev/TEST-SMELLS.md` §4, „Depends on today's date“. | UEB-02 broke on 24.10.2026 with the demo set a month old — four gaps where the widget was built on five; it found a real defect (F-42) only because the calendar happened to move there. — this commit |

The order lives in the address (`sort`, `richtung`; the Protokoll's `reihenfolge`), is kept in every page link, is left out where it is the plain list's, is no filter („gefiltert“ and „Zurücksetzen“ ignore it), and an order the list does not know is refused in German. | D-344 | 2026-10-29 | **Stars typed in the admin, per rim, stand under its name beside the sentence that says where they come from (the client's #19 as we had told him; the operator's decision of 29.10.2026).** Katalog › Felgen › one Felge › „Sterne von Hand“: the stars (1,0 to 5,0, one decimal), the number of reviews behind them and the origin sentence — all three or none. The shop has checked none of them, so the sentence may not say it has: a word such as „verifiziert“, „geprüft“ or „echte“ is refused unless a denial stands within three words before it („von uns nicht geprüft“). Who may change and publish the catalogue may set them (`WheelModelPolicy::rate`) — they go straight into the shop; a demo rim may carry them, so the acceptance rim tries them off production. One Protokoll line per change, its own PUT and DELETE beside the Felge's form. | D-361 | 2026-10-29 | **Stripe issues the invoice for every order paid through it, and a daily copy goes to the shop's S3 bucket (his admin item 17, part 2).** The payment is made for a Stripe customer of the order's own (billing name, e-mail, address, German), so Stripe can attach it: once paid, a draft in euro and A4, one line item per row of the order (InvoiceRows — the same rows as the payment's metadata, D-355) with the account's inclusive 19 % VAT rate (none for a Kleinunternehmer's order), its total checked to the cent against the order (otherwise deleted), finalized without collecting, the succeeded PaymentIntent attached — paid. Every step has its idempotency key and the draft's id is kept, so a retry (five, over about three hours) continues and never makes a second invoice. The confirmation page and the mail link it only once paid; the mail's subject says „und Rechnung“ only then, and „Testkauf“ only for a test purchase (with the own invoices off, release 22 called every real order a test purchase). `rimify:invoices:export-s3` daily 03:15 Berlin: every invoice of the day before, paged to the last, PDF and JSON to `invoices/YYYY/MM/DD/<number>`, each checked by the size the bucket lists (the IAM user may only write and list), twice changes nothing, three tries, then alerted by mail (on by default), audited and shown on System. | The brief: „use the way Stripe documents for that integration … do not invent parameters“ — docs.stripe.com/api/invoices/attach_payment and /invoicing/partial-payments („same currency and customer“). Proven against Stripe's API in memory; the test-mode proof needs his test keys. — `06abcef1` |
